A\r\n  second run started beside a live first one produces failures that read as the subject's â€” a\r\n  publish chain relaunched over a live one reports `EOTP` and `E403` that are its own processes\r\n  colliding, and both readings point at the registry.\r\n- Never ask `pgrep -f` or `ps | grep` whether a command is running from a shell whose own command line\r\n  contains that command's text. The shell matches itself, so the answer is yes whatever the truth is.\r\n  This bites in separate places and each one fails differently:\r\n  - **A liveness watcher** loops forever, reporting \"still running\" and never delivering its completion\r\n    notification, because it always finds itself.\r\n  - **An elapsed-time reading** returns the watcher's age rather than the exec's, so the number falls\r\n    instead of rising and reads as a relaunch that never happened.\r\n  - **A pre-launch \"is anything already running\" check** reports a phantom concurrent writer, which is\r\n    the worst of them: the honest response to it is to kill something, and there is nothing there.\r\n    Read liveness from the recorded process id with `kill -0 <pid>`, or enumerate by executable name and\r\n    parent with `ps -eo pid,ppid,comm` and read the rows. Both are immune; a pattern over the full command\r\n    line is not.\r\n- Kill by process id, never by pattern. `pkill -f` matches the relaunch that is already starting, so\r\n  the pattern that cleans up the old run kills the new one and the cleanup reads as a launch\r\n  failure.\r\n- A killed `codex exec` is dead only when its process tree is dead: walk the children with\r\n  `ps --ppid` and confirm the `codex-code-mode-host` child is gone. Before dispatching a substitute\r\n  writer, check the owned files' modification times against the baseline â€” a live orphan is still\r\n  writing the tree the substitute is about to own.\r\n- Read a failure against what was running when it happened, not against what you believe was\r\n  running. The check costs one command and is the only thing that separates a real failure from\r\n  self-inflicted contention.\r\n\r\n## Bench laws\r\n\r\nExternal engines widen capacity. They never inherit authority. Treat every bench output as a\r\nproposal or hypothesis until it is verified against source and accepted by the Orchestrator.\r\n\r\nA bench is cross-provider reach only. Never send a model across a bridge when the running harness\r\nhosts it natively.\r\n\r\nA bench exec is a long-running command, so every law under **Long-running commands** binds it too.\r\nThis section adds what is true of a bench and nothing else.\r\n\r\nEvery bridge verifies before running that its CLI resolves and its bench is authenticated, and stops\r\nwith a deviation report naming the fallback when either fails. The role file owns the exact\r\ninvocation, flags, paths, probe, and recovery ladder; these laws bind every bench regardless of\r\ntransport.\r\n\r\n1. **Transport by work class.** Use an MCP transport only for a short interactive exchange â€” one\r\n   bounded question or a follow-up on a live thread, finishing in roughly two minutes. Use the\r\n   journaled CLI for audits, implementation units, and anything else multi-minute. An interrupted\r\n   MCP call loses its session invisibly; a journal survives any client-side failure.\r\n2. **Journal first.** Every bench invocation leaves a tailable on-disk record beside its brief\r\n   under `tmp/<bench>/`: the event stream or output log, and the final answer. Arm exactly one\r\n   Monitor per long exec, filtered to milestones â€” commands run, files changed, agent messages,\r\n   terminal states â€” never the raw event stream. Exit the filter on the exec's terminal event so no\r\n   watcher outlives its subject. The journal's mtime is the liveness signal; the session id in its\r\n   head is the recovery handle. The journal is also the proof the bench ran: a bench unit returns\r\n   its journal path and session id with its result, and the Orchestrator confirms both before\r\n   using that result. A report does not carry the engine that produced it, so a bench unit with no\r\n   journal ran on its driver's engine, however normal its answer reads.\r\n3. **Tracked, never loose.** Register every bench unit in the session task registry at launch with\r\n   its subject, journal path, and session id, and complete it there at acceptance. \"What is\r\n   running\" always has a first-class answer instead of a recollection of a command.\r\n4. **One lane at a time per bench.** Launch one `grok` lane at a time and queue the rest. Lanes past\r\n   that starve the bench: it accepts each launch and the lanes come back empty, which reads as the\r\n   work failing. Re-probe before ruling on an empty lane. A probe that round-trips while the lanes\r\n   return empty names starvation, not darkness â€” cut the concurrency and re-run the lane, rather\r\n   than recording the bench dark and substituting an engine.\r\n5. **A bench sandbox spawns a child and denies that child's child.** Under `workspace-write` a bench\r\n   exec runs a test suite and spawns children normally, and every operation one level deeper fails:\r\n   a grandchild process is denied `EPERM`, and a nested `npm install` is denied the same way. So a\r\n   proof needing a process tree, a tree-kill, a detached group, or an installed package cannot be\r\n   produced inside a bench unit at all â€” however carefully that unit isolates. Name the limit in the\r\n   brief before dispatch, tell the unit to record such a proof as an observation naming the exact\r\n   settling command, and take that proof yourself on the host. Never let a unit substitute the\r\n   reachable half: linking a packed tarball is not installing it, and a gate written to catch an\r\n   install failure that only ever links cannot see the defect it exists for.\r\n   Not every symptom names the sandbox. A nested process and a nested install fail `EPERM`, which\r\n   reads as a denial; a nested `git` invocation instead reports **\"not a git repository\"** while the\r\n   unit's own `git status` succeeds a moment earlier. That one reads as a broken checkout, and a unit\r\n   acting on it will go looking for damage that is not there. Tell a unit which of its tools shell out\r\n   one level down â€” a scaffolding CLI that probes git, a formatter that spawns a worker â€” so it\r\n   recognises the shape instead of diagnosing the tree.\r\n   The child a bench does create has unreliable stdio, and that failure wears a worse disguise than a\r\n   denial. A Node process spawned by a bench unit's own Node process has been measured both buffering\r\n   its pipe until EOF and publishing nothing at all, so no workaround built on either reading is\r\n   dependable. Any subject whose behaviour lives in a child's pipes is therefore unmeasurable inside a\r\n   bench: a stage driving a language server, a protocol fixture, a built entry driven as a spawned\r\n   child. It fails as a **false green**. The stage never arms, the boot inspection times out, and that\r\n   timeout produces the same rejection a genuine stage timeout produces, so a test asserting on the\r\n   message passes inside the bench while the host's gate reports the honest red â€” and neither run\r\n   reports why they disagree. Route such a subject to the harness's native implementer, or keep it on\r\n   the bench and supply every executed measurement yourself. Never dispatch it to a bench and expect\r\n   it to prove its own work. The shape to recognise is a child that exits 0 almost immediately, a\r\n   request to it that never resolves, and a stack landing in the spawning code's exit handler.\r\n   **When a bench sandbox denies a loopback listener, `listen` fails `EPERM` on every address.** A\r\n   subject needing a real local server is unmeasurable inside the bench. Name the limit in the brief\r\n   before dispatch. Have the unit report the reading as an observation naming the exact command.\r\n   Take the proof on the host.\r\n   **When a brief assigns a bench unit a path outside the obvious source tree, name the write limit\r\n   in the brief.** If the sandbox rejects the patch, the unit stops and reports the rejection. Never\r\n   find another write mechanism.\r\n   **A bench unit writes only under its own `--cd` root and the system temporary directory.** A\r\n   report path in the orchestrator's repository is outside both, so the write is rejected after the\r\n   work is already done and the unit improvises a fallback the Orchestrator has to go find. Name the\r\n   report path inside the unit's own tree, or tell the unit to return the report as its final\r\n   message and take it from the `--output-last-message` file. Retention still lands the report in\r\n   `.orkestrel/<package>/` â€” the Orchestrator copies it there, and the unit never writes it there.\r\n6. **Ephemeral streams, durable records.** A journal proves a bench is alive and recovers an\r\n   interrupted session. Keep journals under `tmp/`, never commit them, and sweep them at acceptance\r\n   after the final gate evidence is recorded. Durable retention â€” brief, distillate, verdict,\r\n   instrument, acceptance evidence â€” is owned by **Dispatch anatomy**; this rule owns only the\r\n   journal stream.\r\n\r\n### Recovering a dark bench\r\n\r\n- A probe that finds no bench binary records the bench dark and, in the same turn, names to the user\r\n  the install command and the bench it unblocks. Re-probe when the user answers.\r\n- A probe that finds a bench binary present but authentication unavailable starts recovery in the\r\n  same turn. Do not record the bench dark and wait.\r\n- Background the login command with its output captured under `tmp/<bench>/`, surface the\r\n  verification URL and one-time code to the user the moment they appear there, arm a watcher on\r\n  completion, and re-probe when it fires. The bench comes live mid-session with no restart.\r\n- A session that sits dark until the user asks for the login has failed the probe, not the bench.\r\n- Never substitute an API key, access token, copied auth file, or another login flow. If recovery\r\n  cannot complete, record the bench dark, name the fallback in the plan, and say so.\r\n- The role file owns each bench's exact login command and probe.\r\n\r\n## Publishing the fleet\r\n\r\nPublishing is the user's decision and the user's credential. The Orchestrator prepares, surfaces\r\nthe approval, and runs the publishes the user asked for. It never substitutes an API key, an access\r\ntoken, a copied auth file, or another login flow, and it never asks the user to paste a token into\r\nthe conversation.\r\n\r\nA publish chain is a long-running command, so every law under **Long-running commands** binds it:\r\nwrite the chain to a file, detach it with `setsid`, and confirm the previous one is dead before\r\nstarting another. Publish serially, because concurrent publishes collide on the authentication\r\nhandshake and fail each other.\r\n\r\nThe release itself runs from the `orkestrel-publish` skill: the wave's per-repo visit and its bump\r\ntriggers in `references/wave.md`, and the preparation order, the login approval, and the\r\nfive-minute upload window in `references/window.md`. Load the skill when the user asks for a\r\nrelease, and follow it there rather than reconstructing the procedure here. What remains in this\r\nsection binds an executor who is not publishing.\r\n\r\nA wave over unpublished tips derives its order per run from the graph and records only the round\r\neach package landed in, never the order itself.\r\n\r\n### Fixing a dependency before it publishes\r\n\r\nA defect a consumer meets sometimes lives in a package the consumer only has from the registry.\r\nWaiting for that package to publish before the consumer can prove its own fix serializes releases\r\nthat could have been one. Do not wait, and do not work around it in the consumer.\r\n\r\nBuild the dependency from source, pack it, and **install the tarball** into the consumer.\r\n\r\n- **Install it, never link it.** A link resolves through a directory and skips the packing, the\r\n  `files` list, and the exports map â€” which is most of what a distribution proof exists to check. A\r\n  gate written to catch an install failure that only ever linked cannot see the defect it exists for.\r\n- **Write the swap to a script and run the file**, so the build, the pack, and the install are one\r\n  artifact the next run reuses rather than a command nobody can read back.\r\n- **Record the range you replaced** in the same step that replaces it. A consumer sitting on an\r\n  unpublished tarball with no record of what it had is a consumer nobody can restore.\r\n- **Rebuild and repack whenever the source moves.** A stale tarball is the same defect as a stale\r\n  `dist/`, and it is worse for being invisible: the consumer's gates go green against a fix that no\r\n  longer exists in the dependency's tree.\r\n- **Run one unit per checkout, at that checkout's catalog layer.** Give a checkout with no rows an\r\n  adopt unit only when its typecheck against the staged closure reddens.\r\n- **Fetch and merge the dependency's default branch before packing it**, wherever another session\r\n  can move that branch. A pack from a stale tip ships the consumer a dependency the dependency's own\r\n  repository no longer has.\r\n- **Restore the registry copy before any gate that must prove the published artifact, and before\r\n  publishing anything.** A distribution proof run against a local tarball proves the local tarball.\r\n  The release still follows layer order: the dependency publishes first, then the consumer re-pins to\r\n  the version the registry now serves and re-runs its gates against that.\r\n- **Keep the tarballs out of the tree.** They belong under `tmp/`, they are swept at acceptance, and\r\n  they are never committed.\r\n\r\nThe tarball is a head start, not a shortcut. It lets the consumer's work proceed and its proofs run\r\nagainst the real packed artifact while the dependency's own release is still being prepared.\r\n\r\n### What a bump obliges\r\n\r\nA runtime dependency and a development dependency have different blast radius, and confusing them\r\neither publishes packages nobody needed to publish or leaves a consumer pinned to an older release.\r\n\r\n- A **runtime** `dependencies` bump reaches every consumer of the published package. Every package\r\n  downstream of it re-pins, re-runs its gates, bumps, and republishes, in layer order.\r\n- A **development** `devDependencies` bump reaches nobody. Re-pin it, prove the gates still green,\r\n  and commit to `main`. Do not bump the version and do not publish.\r\n- A development bump that moves the published artifact is no longer a development bump. Prove the\r\n  direction with the build, not the diff of sources: rebuild after the re-pin and compare `dist/`\r\n  against the published tarball. Compare material content only â€” exclude sourcemaps and ignore\r\n  whitespace-only differences; a superfluous diff (formatting, blank lines, map noise) moves\r\n  nothing and obliges nothing. A material diff â€” tokens, declarations, logic â€” means the published\r\n  surface moved â€” a forced `src` or `app` edit and a toolchain-changed emit both surface here â€” so\r\n  that package bumps and publishes on its own account, and its own dependents follow the preceding\r\n  runtime rule.\r\n\r\nEvery package is `0.0.x`, where a caret pins one exact release. A dependent therefore sees a new\r\nversion only after it re-pins and republishes, so the fleet publishes in topological layer order\r\nderived from the runtime `dependencies` and `peerDependencies` edges, never from a development\r\nedge. Layers exist for a reason a flat pass cannot fix: ranges that disagree install duplicate\r\ncopies of the same package, and the compiler reads them as distinct types.\r\n\r\nRead the order from the catalog table in `.claude/agents/orkestrel.md`, which `scaffold catalog`\r\nregenerates from the registry. Its `Layer` column is the publish round. Regenerate it before\r\nsequencing a cascade rather than trusting the copy in the tree, and never write a second order down\r\nsomewhere else.\r\n\r\nThe tooling packages sit outside that order because nothing depends on them at runtime. `scaffold`\r\nis a development dependency of every package, including packages it depends on itself, so a runtime\r\nlayering would report a cycle that does not exist. Each package builds against the already-published\r\n`scaffold`, never against an unpublished one, and a `scaffold` release therefore publishes on its own\r\nand propagates as files rather than as a cascade. A package the fleet consumes as a development\r\ndependency takes the same shape when its consumers' gates read its unpublished tip;\r\n`.agents/skills/orkestrel-publish/references/wave.md` Â§ Rule on the bump states that trigger.\r\n\r\n`scaffold` also carries a second published surface beside `dist/src`: `package.json` ships\r\n`dist/host`, the vendored file set every target receives through `repair`.\r\n\r\n- Bump and publish `scaffold` when any vendored byte changes, or when the set of vendored paths\r\n  changes. That surface moved on its own account, and `dist/src` need not move with it.\r\n- Re-pin `@orkestrel/scaffold` in each target after a vendored-only release, run `repair` there, and\r\n  prove that target's gates still green. `repair` restores `tests/setupPolicy.ts` and\r\n  `tests/policy.test.ts`, so a vendored-only release can turn a green target red. A target bumps\r\n  only when its own published surface moved.\r\n- Keep a target's own Claude permissions in `.claude/settings.local.json`, never in the vendored\r\n  `.claude/settings.json`. `repair` restores the vendored copy, so a `defaultMode` or an `allow`\r\n  entry added there is reverted without warning and the operator loses grants they set\r\n  deliberately. Change the vendored file only here, in the host inventory.\r\n- Never edit a vendored file inside a target. `repair` restores it, so the edit is reverted and\r\n  reports as drift in `scaffold audit`. In this repository those same files are the published\r\n  `dist/host` surface, so editing one forces a bump, a publish, and a re-propagation across every\r\n  target. no `any`, assertion, or\r\nnon-null assertion anywhere in the unit:\r\n\r\n```ts\r\nexport interface ProviderParserInterface<TRecord = Readonly<Record<string, unknown>>> {\r\n\tparse(chunk: string): ProviderIncrement\r\n\tfinish(parser: ProviderParserInterface<TRecord>): readonly TRecord[]\r\n}\r\n\r\nexport type ProviderErrorCode = 'HTTP' | 'PROTOCOL' | 'LIMIT' | 'PROVIDER'\r\n\r\nexport result: ProviderResult }\r\n\t| { readonly channel: 'abort'; readonly partial: ProviderResult }\r\n\t| { readonly channel: 'error'; readonly code: 'PROVIDER'; readonly message: string }\r\n\r\nexport Promise<boolean>\r\n\treadonly limit?: number\r\n}\r\n\r\nexport interface RelayStreamOptions {\r\n\treadonly provider: ProviderInterface\r\n\treadonly request: ProviderRequest\r\n\treadonly signal: AbortSignal\r\n}\r\n\r\nexport interface RelayProviderOptions extends ProviderOptions {\r\n\treadonly url: string\r\n\treadonly frame: () => ProviderParserInterface\r\n}\r\n```\r\n\r\nThe relay types are declared now so the contract is complete; unit A2 implements them. `body`\r\nreturns `object` because a wire body is an interface value (ollama's `WireChatRequest`) that an\r\nindex-signature type would refuse; the base owns `JSON.stringify` of it.\r\n\r\n**Semantics the base owns** (each one is a test you write):\r\n\r\n1. `id` is a fresh `crypto.randomUUID()` per instance; `format` is exposed exactly as given\r\n   (`undefined` when omitted). `name` is an abstract readonly member the subclass declares.\r\n2. Each call arms one `Timeout` for `timeout ?? DEFAULT_PROVIDER_TIMEOUT` and folds\r\n   `AbortSignal.any([timeout.signal, signal])`; the timeout is cleared on every exit path —\r\n   success, failure, and abort — never left armed. Prove it through a recorded transport's signal\r\n   that stays unaborted after the deadline would have fired.\r\n3. The default transport is `globalThis.fetch` bound to `globalThis` (a bare reference through a\r\n   field throws `Illegal invocation` in a browser). Prove the receiver: a provider built with no\r\n   `fetch` option invokes the global with `globalThis` as `this`.\r\n4. The `headers` hook is awaited inside the bounded region and raced against the combined signal,\r\n   so a hook that never resolves rejects when the deadline fires; its entries merge over\r\n   `Content-Type: application/json` (it may override the content type only by returning one).\r\n5. One `POST` of `JSON.stringify(this.body(request))` to `url + (path ?? '')` with the combined\r\n   signal. A non-OK status reads at most `MAX_ERROR_BODY_LENGTH` characters of the body through\r\n   `readText`, cancels the remainder, and throws `ProviderError('HTTP', message, { status })` with\r\n   the message `provider error: <status> - <excerpt>`; a body-read failure throws the same code and\r\n   status with `(error body unavailable)` and the `cause`. A `null` body throws\r\n   `ProviderError('PROTOCOL', …)`.\r\n6. The body is decoded through `readChunks` (a streaming `TextDecoder`, final flush, reader\r\n   cancelled in `finally`) and framed by one `frame()` parser per call; each record goes through\r\n   `read`. At end of input the records `finish(parser)` returns go through `read` too, then the\r\n   splitter is flushed and any held tail is yielded as the final content delta. `parser.clear()`\r\n   runs in `finally`.\r\n7. When `split` is `true` (the default) every record's raw `content` routes through one\r\n   `createThinkSplitter()` per call and only clean content is yielded as `{ channel: 'content' }`;\r\n   the splitter's authoritative `content` is the assembled result's content (across the qwen3\r\n   implicit-open reclassification the result stays clean while already-yielded deltas cannot be\r\n   recalled — keep that documented behaviour). When `split` is `false` raw content is yielded and\r\n   accumulated verbatim. Native `thinking` is yielded as `{ channel: 'thinking' }` and joined\r\n   through `joinThinking` with the splitter's separated spans. `tools` append. A present `usage`\r\n   replaces the running one; an absent one keeps it.\r\n8. A present `result` on an increment is the authoritative settled result: `stream` returns it\r\n   unchanged (no re-assembly, no double count). When `strict` is `true` and end of input arrives\r\n   with no `result`, throw `ProviderError('PROTOCOL', …)`.\r\n9. A cancel of the combined signal mid-stream throws `ProviderAbortError` carrying the partial\r\n   assembled so far, with the splitter flushed first so held clean content is included. Any other\r\n   throw — a `ProviderAbortError` thrown by `read` for a remotely reported abort included —\r\n   propagates unchanged. An already-aborted signal rejects before any request is issued.\r\n10. `generate` drains `stream` with explicit `next()` calls and returns the terminal value, so\r\n    `generate` deep-equals a drained `stream` on the same input. No subclass overrides it.\r\n11. Concurrent calls on one instance share nothing: parser, splitter, timeout, and accumulators\r\n    are per call.\r\n\r\n**Errors** (`src/core/errors.ts`): `ProviderError extends Error` with `readonly code: ProviderErrorCode`,\r\n`readonly status: number | undefined`, `name = 'ProviderError'`, `cause` carried through\r\n`ErrorOptions`, constructor `(code, message, options?)`; `isProviderError` narrows through\r\n`instanceof`. Follow the `ProviderAbortError` block's TSDoc form.\r\n\r\n**Constants** (`src/core/constants.ts`): `DEFAULT_PROVIDER_TIMEOUT = 120_000`,\r\n`MAX_ERROR_BODY_LENGTH = 2048`, `DEFAULT_RELAY_LIMIT = 1_048_576`,\r\n`RELAY_CONTENT_TYPE = 'application/x-ndjson; charset=utf-8'`.\r\n\r\n**Helpers** (`src/core/helpers.ts`): `buildProviderResult(content, thinking, tools, usage)` (moved\r\nfrom ollama, omitting empty `thinking`, empty `tools`, and absent `usage`); `joinThinking` widened\r\nso `''` on either side is treated as absent (`joinThinking('', 'x')` → `'x'`,\r\n`joinThinking('x', '')` → `'x'`), behaviour-preserving for its existing callers;\r\n`readText(body: ReadableStream<Uint8Array>, limit?: number): Promise<string>` (decodes, stops at\r\n`limit` bytes, cancels the remainder, returns what it read); `readChunks(body): AsyncGenerator<string>`.\r\nEvery helper is a pure leaf, exported, and tested.\r\n\r\n**Shapes and contracts** (the user's instruction). Lifecycle verbs have fixed meanings.\r\n- **Boolean behavior.** A binary behavioral switch is a boolean, not a two-literal union.\r\n- **Real domain states only.** Literal unions represent irreducible modes, phases, discriminants, or external values — not decorative labels for facts already represented.\r\n- **Absence is `undefined`.** Never invent sentinels such as `'none'`, `'unset'`, `'unknown'`, `''`, or `-1`. Use `null` only when an external format distinguishes it from omission.\r\n- **Derive state.** Compute facts from existing fields. Keep stateful or defining orchestration as class methods. Classes compose behavior; they do not forward 1:1 to helpers.\r\n- **No superfluous wrappers.** A wrapper must add a boundary, invariant, composition, translation, lifecycle, or materially narrower contract. Prefer one minimal interface and one shared engine, allowing native backend overrides only for genuine faster paths.\r\n- **No compatibility shims.** This is greenfield. Shape:\r\n\r\n- `Touched files`: each with one line on what changed and why; `git diff --stat` output.\r\n- `Contract`: the final `types.ts` additions verbatim.\r\n- `Red then green`: for every defect-shaped test — the `isMessage` cases, the `joinThinking`\r\n  widening, the bounded error read, the hook race — the exact command, its failing count before,\r\n  and its passing count after.\r\n- `Scoped validation`: the exact commands run (`npm run lint:check`, `npm run check:src:core`,\r\n  `npm run test:src:core`, `npm run test:setup`) with their exit codes and counts.\r\n- `Observations`: `npm run test:guides` reading; the worker-spawn reading; the `contracts.ts`\r\n  naming ruling; anything you measured that the Orchestrator should know.\r\n- `Deviation`: `none`, or the stop report.\r\n- `Status`: `git status --porcelain` verbatim.\r\n\r\nNo process diary.\r\n\r\n## Deviation contract\r\n\r\nStop and report — expected, found, exact evidence, done or not done, one short hypothesis — when\r\nthe ruled contract cannot compile under the scoped config as written, when an installed\r\n`@orkestrel/*` declaration contradicts a fact in this brief, or when a rule in the Law section\r\nforbids something this brief instructs. Decide, record, and carry on from any question of test\r\nnaming, file ordering, or TSDoc wording.\r\n\r\n## Acceptance criteria\r\n\r\n1. `npm run lint:check` exits 0.\r\n2. `npm run check:src:core` exits 0; `npm run check` exits 0.\r\n3. Every declaration in § Contract exists in `src/core/types.ts` with the stated members and is\r\n   exported from the barrel; `AgentProvider`, `ProviderError`, `isProviderError`,\r\n   `buildProviderResult`, `readText`, `readChunks`, the shapes, and the compiled contracts are\r\n   exported from the barrel.\r\n4. `npm run test:src:core` and `npm run test:setup` exit 0 with every new test file collected\r\n   (name each file and its test count), or the worker-spawn standing condition is recorded verbatim\r\n   and the suite result is left to the Orchestrator.\r\n5. `isMessage` rejects `{ id: '1', role: 'other', content: '' }` and\r\n   `{ id: '1', role: 'user', content: '', images: [1] }`; red-then-green recorded.\r\n6. `joinThinking('', 'x')` returns `'x'`; red-then-green recorded.\r\n7. A non-OK response whose body exceeds `MAX_ERROR_BODY_LENGTH` bytes is read to at most that many\r\n   bytes before the stream is cancelled; red-then-green recorded through a recorded stream.\r\n8. A `headers` hook that never resolves rejects with the deadline's abort and leaves no armed\r\n   timer; red-then-green recorded.\r\n9. No `any`, `as` (other than `as const` on a literal field), `!`, `@ts-ignore`,\r\n   `@ts-expect-error`, `eslint-disable`, `oxlint-disable`, `public`, `protected`, `private`, or\r\n   parameter property anywhere in the diff; no module-scope declaration in `AgentProvider.ts`\r\n   beside the class; no nested function declaration.\r\n\r\n**Observations, not criteria.** `npm run test:guides` (red is expected until A3); the full\r\n`npm test` chain and `npm run build` (the Orchestrator's).\r\n\r\n## Review evidence\r\n\r\nA code change: the report carries `git diff --stat` and `git status --porcelain`; the Orchestrator\r\ntakes the full diff from the tree.\r\n# Design reconciliation — provider base, `OllamaProvider` on it, browser↔server relay\r\n\r\nRuled by the Orchestrator on 2026-09-14 from two blind lanes on one brief\r\n(`design-brief.md`): `design-subjective-report.md` (planner, Opus 5) and\r\n`design-objective-report.md` (analyst, GPT 6 Astra). Neither lane saw the other. Findings the\r\nlanes disagreed on were reproduced before ruling where a probe could settle them.\r\n\r\n## Agreed by both lanes, adopted\r\n\r\n- The base is an abstract class named `AgentProvider` in `@orkestrel/agent` `src/core`; concrete\r\n  providers `extends` it. It is host-independent and HTTP-shaped. One base, no transport-agnostic\r\n  superclass until a process-shaped consumer exists.\r\n- The framing seam is a structural `ProviderParserInterface<TRecord>` with `parse` and `clear`,\r\n  satisfied by the installed `NDJSONParserInterface` and `SSEParserInterface` without agent\r\n  depending on either package. The concrete provider supplies the parser.\r\n- `@orkestrel/ollama` moves from a server face to a core face; the bare specifier does not change.\r\n  Node fixture servers stay in `tests/setupServer.ts` and are imported directly by test files, as\r\n  the service suites already do. Probed: the vendored `tests/config.test.ts:120-125` pins the\r\n  `src:core` project to `setupFiles: ['./tests/setup.ts']`, and the tests lint override restricts\r\n  only the `typescript` import, so a direct fixture import is permitted.\r\n- The relay is both kinds: the transparent wire proxy stays as a documented pattern, and an\r\n  agent-level relay is added — `createRelay` returning a host-independent\r\n  `(request: Request) => Promise<Response>` handler mountable on `@orkestrel/router`, and a\r\n  browser-side `RelayProvider`. The request carries identified `Message` values, `tools`, and\r\n  `options`; the response is NDJSON; the terminal frame carries the authoritative `ProviderResult`\r\n  so `thinking`, `tools`, and `usage` survive the hop; a server-side abort becomes an abort frame\r\n  the browser rethrows as `ProviderAbortError`.\r\n- `authorize` on the server half is mandatory; token minting, validation, and identity stay in the\r\n  application. The browser half authenticates through the existing `headers` hook.\r\n- `buildResult` moves to agent; `OllamaResponse` is retired; ollama's `joinThinking` is deleted in\r\n  favour of the agent's (probed: the two functions have different signatures and disagree on\r\n  empty input).\r\n- Publish agent `0.0.22` first, then ollama `0.0.16` re-pinned to `^0.0.22`; toolbox re-pins in\r\n  its own later campaign. During the campaign the agent artifact reaches ollama as an installed\r\n  packed tarball, never a link.\r\n- No browser Vitest project and no new dependency.\r\n\r\n## Rulings on the disagreements\r\n\r\n| # | Question | Subjective lane | Objective lane | Ruling and reason |\r\n| - | -------- | --------------- | -------------- | ----------------- |\r\n| 1 | Seam form | Abstract methods on the subclass (`body`, `read`) plus a public `request`; `url`, `path`, `frame` as constructor input | A composed `wire` object exposed as a readonly property, with `finish`, `error`, `unary`, `split`, `strict` | **Abstract methods on the subclass, with the objective lane's seam set.** The subclass fills `frame`, `body`, `read`, and `finish`; `url`, `path`, `split`, and `strict` are constructor input; `name` is an abstract data member. A wire object would leave `OllamaProvider` an empty subclass, which the wrapper test forbids. `request` stays `#`-private: nothing outside the base needs it once `generate` is ruled below. The objective lane was right that end-of-input is wire-specific (`finish`), and that the relay must not re-split (`split`) and must require a terminal frame (`strict`). |\r\n| 2 | `generate` | Drains `stream`; Ollama overrides with its native `stream: false` call | Base selects a native unary path when the wire supplies a decoder | **`generate` drains `stream`, and nobody overrides it.** One engine, exactly as `Agent.generate()` drains `Agent.stream()`. Ollama's `stream: false` mode is not a faster path, only a second flow; its guide clause and the request-shape assertion that pin `stream: false` are ours to change and are recorded as a release change. This also removes the multi-line-JSON hazard that framing a unary body through an NDJSON parser would have introduced. |\r\n| 3 | HTTP error | Shared `ProviderHTTPError` in agent; ollama's error class removed (flagged as the user's call) | Wire-specific `error(failure)` translation keeping `OllamaHTTPError` | **One shared `ProviderError` in agent with `code: 'HTTP' \\| 'PROTOCOL' \\| 'LIMIT' \\| 'PROVIDER'` and an optional `status`; `OllamaHTTPError`, `isOllamaHTTPError`, and `OllamaHTTPErrorOptions` are removed.** The only vendor-specific content of ollama's error is the word \"Ollama\" in its message. A per-vendor error seam would be a wrapper. Recorded for the user as a public removal they can reverse. |\r\n| 4 | Error-body bound | Slice after `response.text()` (as today) | Stop reading after a bounded prefix and cancel the remainder | **Objective lane.** Reproduced: `OllamaProvider.ts:370` reads the whole error body before slicing, which does not bound the read. The base reads at most `MAX_ERROR_BODY_LENGTH` and cancels the rest. |\r\n| 5 | Body readers | Inline reader loop in the base | A `ProviderReader` class with limits and truncation shared by base and relay | **Two exported helpers rather than a class:** `readText(body, limit?)` collects a bounded text and cancels the remainder, and `readChunks(body)` yields decoded text with a final flush and a cancel in `finally`. Both are pure leaves over a `ReadableStream`; the relay handler reuses `readText` to bound its request body. |\r\n| 6 | Relay frame vocabulary | `channel` discriminant reusing `ProviderDelta` as-is; `settle`, `abort`, `error` arms | `event` discriminant with a `sequence` number and a `version: 1` request field | **`channel`, reusing `ProviderDelta` verbatim; arms `result`, `abort`, `error`; no `sequence`, no `version`.** One discriminant name for one concept. Loss detection buys little because the `result` frame is authoritative for the final value and `strict` already fails end-of-input without it; a version field on a `0.0.x` greenfield wire is speculation. |\r\n| 7 | `authorize` return | `boolean`; false answers `401` | `Response \\| undefined`; the application supplies the denial | **`boolean`.** Anything richer composes in front as ordinary `@orkestrel/server` middleware, which is the line's documented pattern for MCP. |\r\n| 8 | Request body bound on the server | Not addressed | Finite configurable limits | **Adopted for the request:** `RelayOptions.limit` in bytes, default `1_048_576`, answered with `413`. Provider success bodies stay unbounded as today and the guide says so. |\r\n| 9 | JSON representability across the hop | Not addressed | Reject unrepresentable values before dispatch; do not silently drop `caller` | **Adopted.** `RelayProvider.body` projects the request through a `providerRequestToJSON` helper gated by contract's JSON guards, exactly as `agentResultToJSON` already does, and throws `ProviderError('PROTOCOL')` when the value is not JSON. `ToolCall.caller` never crosses the hop and the guide states it (it is declared `unknown`, consumer-asserted context). |\r\n| 10 | `isMessage` | Not addressed | The guard is weaker than the type; fix it, do not add a second guard | **Adopted as a repair with red/green.** Reproduced at `validators.ts:36-42`: any `role` string and any `images` array pass. The guard now narrows to the `MessageRole` union and string image elements. A stored snapshot carrying a role outside the union now reads as absent, which is the fail-closed reading the guard already claims for `calls`. |\r\n| 11 | Placement | `providers/AgentProvider.ts` | `AgentProvider.ts` at the module root | **Root for the defining base, `providers/` for concrete providers:** `agent/src/core/AgentProvider.ts`, `agent/src/core/providers/RelayProvider.ts`, `agent/src/core/RelayStream.ts`; `ollama/src/core/OllamaProvider.ts` stays where its class sits today. |\r\n| 12 | Browser proof | Core-scoped typecheck plus a bound-`fetch` runtime assertion; state the limit | The same, plus a real Chromium smoke through the available harness | **Both.** C3 closes on the mechanical evidence and on a Chromium receipt the Orchestrator takes with the harness browser against the built exports, retained under `.orkestrel/campaign/`. No persistent browser project. |\r\n| 13 | Scope list omissions | — | `guides/README.md` and the lockfiles are outside the may-change list | **Adopted.** Both packages' `guides/README.md` and ollama's `package-lock.json` join the scope. |\r\n| 14 | Unresolved `headers` hook under abort | Not addressed | A timeout signal handed to `fetch` cannot interrupt an unresolved hook promise | **Adopted.** The base races the hook against the combined signal and proves it with a never-resolving hook. |\r\n| 15 | `usage` gating | Fold whatever `read` reports | A usage frame replaces the prior report | **Same behaviour, adopted as `ProviderIncrement.usage?`:** present replaces, absent keeps. Ollama's `extractUsage` already returns `undefined` unless both counts are present, so its observable behaviour is unchanged. |\r\n\r\n## Which lane was right on what\r\n\r\n- The subjective lane was right on the seam form, the discriminant, the `authorize` shape, the\r\n  name set, the `joinThinking` collision, and the unit decomposition.\r\n- The objective lane was right on `finish`, `split`, `strict`, the unbounded error read, the\r\n  request-body bound, JSON representability, the `isMessage` defect, the unresolved-hook hazard,\r\n  the scope omissions, and the Chromium receipt.\r\n- The Orchestrator overruled both on `generate` and on the error taxonomy, for the reasons in the\r\n  table.\r\n\r\n## Decisions made on the user's behalf, reversible\r\n\r\n1. `OllamaHTTPError`, `isOllamaHTTPError`, `OllamaHTTPErrorOptions`, `MAX_ERROR_BODY_LENGTH`,\r\n   `DEFAULT_PROVIDER_TIMEOUT`, `buildResult`, `joinThinking`, `parseBody`, and `OllamaResponse`\r\n   leave `@orkestrel/ollama`; the first five reappear in `@orkestrel/agent` as `ProviderError`,\r\n   `isProviderError`, `MAX_ERROR_BODY_LENGTH`, `DEFAULT_PROVIDER_TIMEOUT`, and\r\n   `buildProviderResult`.\r\n2. `OllamaProvider.generate` no longer sends `stream: false`; it drains the stream.\r\n3. `@orkestrel/ollama` publishes a core face instead of a server face.\r\n4. `@orkestrel/agent` gains no runtime dependency; a relay browser app passes\r\n   `createNDJSONParser` from `@orkestrel/ndjson` to `RelayProvider` itself.\r\n# Plan — provider base, `OllamaProvider` on it, browser↔server relay\r\n\r\nStatus: design reconciled 2026-09-14 (see `design-reconciliation.md`); implementation units\r\ndispatching. `registry.md` holds the routing ledger and bench state. This file is rewritten at each\r\nre-baseline.\r\n\r\n## Enumerated scope (fixed 2026-09-14 at campaign start)\r\n\r\n| Capability | Closes when                                                                                                                                                                                                                       | State |\r\n| ---------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----- |\r\n| C1         | `@orkestrel/agent` core exports a host-independent provider base that owns every provider mechanic up to the wire-specific seams, typed in `types.ts`, barrelled, tested in `src:core`, and documented in `agent.md`             | open  |\r\n| C2         | `OllamaProvider` is rebuilt on that base with no duplicated mechanic; ollama's unit, conformance, and service suites are green                                                                                                     | open  |\r\n| C3         | A browser runtime can import and run the provider: host-independence proven under the core scope with no Node types, a bound-`fetch` runtime assertion, and a Chromium receipt driving the built exports                          | open  |\r\n| C4         | A browser-side provider relays to a server-side provider through the developer's server: custom token in, real credential server-side only, deltas streamed back, abort crossing the hop, policy hooks left to the application | open  |\r\n| C5         | Guides, parity, tests, and the gate chain green in agent and ollama; publish order agent then ollama prepared                                                                                                                      | open  |\r\n\r\n## Exit criterion\r\n\r\nEvery row above ends implemented, repaired, retained, or intentionally excluded on evidence, and\r\n`npm run format:check`, `lint:check`, `check`, `build`, and `test` are green in agent and ollama\r\nas run by an independent `verifier`, with ollama's `test:service` green against the warm daemon.\r\nPublishing is the user's decision and outside the criterion.\r\n\r\n## The ruled contract\r\n\r\nAdditions to `agent/src/core/types.ts` (TSDoc omitted here; the unit writes it):\r\n\r\n```ts\r\nexport interface ProviderParserInterface<TRecord = Readonly<Record<string, unknown>>> {\r\n\tparse(chunk: string): readonly TRecord[]\r\n\tclear(): void\r\n}\r\n\r\nexport interface ProviderRequest {\r\n\treadonly messages: readonly Message[]\r\n\treadonly tools?: readonly ToolDefinition[]\r\n\treadonly options?: ProviderStreamOptions\r\n}\r\n\r\nexport interface ProviderIncrement {\r\n\treadonly content: string\r\n\treadonly thinking: string\r\n\treadonly tools: readonly ToolCall[]\r\n\treadonly usage?: TokenUsage\r\n\treadonly result?: ProviderResult\r\n}\r\n\r\nexport interface ProviderOptions {\r\n\treadonly timeout?: number\r\n\treadonly fetch?: typeof globalThis.fetch\r\n\treadonly headers?: () =>\r\n\t\t| Readonly<Record<string, string>>\r\n\t\t| Promise<Readonly<Record<string, string>>>\r\n\treadonly format?: ContextFormat\r\n}\r\n\r\nexport interface AgentProviderInput extends ProviderOptions {\r\n\treadonly url: string\r\n\treadonly path?: string\r\n\treadonly split?: boolean\r\n\treadonly strict?: boolean\r\n}\r\n\r\nexport interface AgentProviderInterface<TRecord = Readonly<Record<string, unknown>>>\r\n\textends ProviderInterface {\r\n\tframe(): ProviderParserInterface<TRecord>\r\n\tbody(request: ProviderRequest): object\r\n\tread(record: TRecord): ProviderIncrement\r\n\tfinish(parser: ProviderParserInterface<TRecord>): readonly TRecord[]\r\n}\r\n\r\nexport type ProviderErrorCode = 'HTTP' | 'PROTOCOL' | 'LIMIT' | 'PROVIDER'\r\n\r\nexport interface ProviderErrorOptions {\r\n\treadonly status?: number\r\n\treadonly cause?: unknown\r\n}\r\n\r\nexport type RelayFrame =\r\n\t| ProviderDelta\r\n\t| { readonly channel: 'result'; readonly result: ProviderResult }\r\n\t| { readonly channel: 'abort'; readonly partial: ProviderResult }\r\n\t| { readonly channel: 'error'; readonly code: 'PROVIDER'; readonly message: string }\r\n\r\nexport type RelayHandler = (request: Request) => Promise<Response>\r\n\r\nexport interface RelayOptions {\r\n\treadonly provider: ProviderInterface\r\n\treadonly authorize: (request: Request) => boolean | Promise<boolean>\r\n\treadonly limit?: number\r\n}\r\n\r\nexport interface RelayStreamOptions {\r\n\treadonly provider: ProviderInterface\r\n\treadonly request: ProviderRequest\r\n\treadonly signal: AbortSignal\r\n}\r\n\r\nexport interface RelayProviderOptions extends ProviderOptions {\r\n\treadonly url: string\r\n\treadonly frame: () => ProviderParserInterface\r\n}\r\n```\r\n\r\nSemantics the base owns: a fresh `crypto.randomUUID()` `id`; `format` exposed as given; per-call\r\n`Timeout` plus `AbortSignal.any([timeout.signal, signal])`; the bound default `fetch` and the\r\nawaited `headers` hook raced against the combined signal, merged over `Content-Type: application/json`;\r\none POST of `JSON.stringify(body(request))` to `url + (path ?? '')`; a non-OK status read to at\r\nmost `MAX_ERROR_BODY_LENGTH` then cancelled and thrown as `ProviderError('HTTP', message, { status })`;\r\na `null` body thrown as `ProviderError('PROTOCOL')`; the response body decoded through `readChunks`\r\nand framed by one `frame()` parser per call; each record through `read`; when `split` (default\r\n`true`) raw `content` goes through one `createThinkSplitter()` per call and clean content is yielded\r\nas `{ channel: 'content' }`; native `thinking` yielded as `{ channel: 'thinking' }`; `tools`\r\nappended; a present `usage` replaces the running one; a present `result` becomes the authoritative\r\nsettled result; at end of input `finish(parser)` is fed through `read` too, the splitter is flushed,\r\nand when `strict` and no `result` arrived a `ProviderError('PROTOCOL')` is thrown; a cancel of the\r\ncombined signal throws `ProviderAbortError` carrying the partial assembled so far; any other error\r\n(a `ProviderAbortError` thrown by `read` for a remote abort included) propagates unchanged; the\r\nreader is cancelled, the parser cleared, and the timeout cleared in `finally`; `generate` drains\r\n`stream` with explicit `next()` calls and returns the terminal value. Both lanes' evidence for each\r\nof these is in `absorb-provider-report.md` item 4 and the two design reports.\r\n\r\nErrors in `agent/src/core/errors.ts`: `ProviderError` (`code: ProviderErrorCode`,\r\n`status: number | undefined`, `cause` through `ErrorOptions`) and `isProviderError`.\r\n\r\nConstants in `agent/src/core/constants.ts`: `DEFAULT_PROVIDER_TIMEOUT = 120_000`,\r\n`MAX_ERROR_BODY_LENGTH = 2048`, `DEFAULT_RELAY_LIMIT = 1_048_576`, and the relay content type.\r\n\r\nHelpers in `agent/src/core/helpers.ts`: `buildProviderResult(content, thinking, tools, usage)`\r\n(from ollama's `buildResult`), `joinThinking` widened so an empty string on either side is treated\r\nas absent, `readText(body, limit?)`, `readChunks(body)`, `providerRequestToJSON(request)`.\r\n\r\nValidators in `agent/src/core/validators.ts`: `isMessage` repaired to the `MessageRole` union and\r\nstring image elements; `isProviderRequest`; `isRelayFrame`.\r\n\r\nFactories in `agent/src/core/factories.ts`: `createRelay(options): RelayHandler`,\r\n`createRelayProvider(options): RelayProvider`.\r\n\r\nClasses: `agent/src/core/AgentProvider.ts` (abstract), `agent/src/core/providers/RelayProvider.ts`\r\n(`name = 'relay'`, `split: false`, `strict: true`, `body` through `providerRequestToJSON`, `read`\r\nnarrows a record with `isRelayFrame`, maps deltas to increments, `result` to the settled result,\r\n`abort` to a thrown `ProviderAbortError(partial)`, `error` to a thrown `ProviderError('PROVIDER')`,\r\nand a bad frame to `ProviderError('PROTOCOL')`; `finish` returns `parser.parse('\\n')`),\r\n`agent/src/core/RelayStream.ts` (a pull-driven `Response` over one `provider.stream` call: one\r\nawaited `next()` per pull, frames as `JSON.stringify(frame) + '\\n'`, a `result` frame on completion,\r\nan `abort` frame for a `ProviderAbortError`, an `error` frame with a fixed message for any other\r\nthrow, the upstream signal aborted then `iterator.return()` on body cancel, listeners released).\r\n\r\n`createRelay` flow: `authorize(request)` false → `401`; `readText(request.body, limit)` over the\r\nlimit → `413`; JSON parse plus `isProviderRequest` failure → `400`; otherwise\r\n`new RelayStream({ provider, request, signal: request.signal }).response` with\r\n`content-type: application/x-ndjson; charset=utf-8` and `cache-control: no-store`.\r\n\r\nOllama after the rebuild: `OllamaProvider extends AgentProvider` with `name = 'ollama'`,\r\n`super({ url: options.url ?? DEFAULT_OLLAMA_URL, path: OLLAMA_CHAT_PATH, ...options })`,\r\n`frame()` returning `createNDJSONParser()`, `body(request)` returning the `WireChatRequest` with\r\n`stream: true`, `read(record)` through `extractContent` / `extractThinking` / `extractTools` /\r\n`extractUsage`, `finish(parser)` returning `parser.parse('\\n')`. `OllamaOptions extends ProviderOptions`\r\nkeeps `model`, `url?`, `keepAlive?`, `options?`, `think?`. Removed from ollama: `OllamaResponse`,\r\n`OllamaHTTPError`, `isOllamaHTTPError`, `OllamaHTTPErrorOptions`, `MAX_ERROR_BODY_LENGTH`,\r\n`DEFAULT_PROVIDER_TIMEOUT`, `buildResult`, `joinThinking`, `parseBody`; `errors.ts` and\r\n`parsers.ts` are deleted when empty. Added: `OLLAMA_CHAT_PATH`.\r\n\r\n## Units and routing ledger\r\n\r\nWriters are serialized per checkout. `agent` and `ollama` are disjoint checkouts, so A-units and\r\nO1 run in parallel; O2 onward waits for the agent artifact.\r\n\r\n| Unit | Subject                                                                  | Role · engine                | Checkout | Depends on | Audit lanes                                        |\r\n| ---- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ---------------------------- | -------- | ---------- | -------------------------------------------------- |\r\n| A1   | Agent contract, `AgentProvider`, errors, constants, helpers, `isMessage` repair, core tests | `sol` · GPT 6 Astra  | agent    | —          | reviewer (Opus 5) decides; analyst (Astra) objective; checker |\r\n| A2   | Relay: `RelayProvider`, `RelayStream`, `createRelay`, guards, projection, tests | `sol` · GPT 6 Astra     | agent    | A1         | reviewer (Opus 5) decides; analyst (Astra); checker |\r\n| A3   | Agent guide, README, `guides/README.md`, parity test                     | `implementer` · Opus 5       | agent    | A2         | analyst (Astra) decides; checker                   |\r\n| A4   | Pack the accepted agent tree; install the tarball into ollama, recording the replaced range | Orchestrator tracked command | agent → ollama | A3   | verifier reads resolution                          |\r\n| O1   | Ollama environment move, server face → core face, no behaviour change   | `builder` · Sonnet           | ollama   | —          | checker; verifier                                  |\r\n| O2   | `OllamaProvider` rebuilt on the base; removals; tests                    | `sol` · GPT 6 Astra          | ollama   | A4, O1     | reviewer (Opus 5) decides; A consumer can predict them without documentation.\r\n\r\n## Entity-scoped names: one word\r\n\r\nProperties/getters, methods, option keys, and event names belong to an entity whose type already supplies context. Use one descriptive word:\r\n\r\n```ts\r\ninterface AgentInterface {\r\n\treadonly id: string\r\n\treadonly context: AgentContextInterface\r\n\treadonly status: AgentStatus\r\n\tgenerate(): Promise<string>\r\n\tstream(options?: AgentStreamOptions): AsyncGenerator<string, string>\r\n\tabort(): void\r\n}\r\n```\r\n\r\nHard targets:\r\n\r\n- Public properties: one word.\r\n- Public methods: one word; use two only when no single verb is accurate.\r\n- Ungrouped option keys: one word.\r\n- Grouped option key: the configured entity noun; every leaf remains one word.\r\n- Events: one present-tense verb or noun.\r\n- Private methods: two or three words are acceptable.\r\n\r\nThe rule does not apply to standalone helpers, type names with role suffixes, or qualified constants.\r\n\r\n## Split instead of compounding\r\n\r\nA compound entity member usually means the API contains multiple entities or behaviors. Do not create `addTool`, `removeDocument`, `databasePath`, or `serverTimeout`.\r\n\r\n### Group options by entity\r\n\r\n```ts\r\ninterface ServerOptions {\r\n\treadonly server?: { readonly port?: number; readonly timeout?: number }\r\n\treadonly database?: { readonly path?: string; readonly timeout?: number }\r\n}\r\n```\r\n\r\nNever flatten these into prefixed keys.\r\n\r\n### Extract sub-entities\r\n\r\nMove prefixed method families to a manager and expose it as a noun:\r\n\r\n```ts\r\ninterface AgentContextInterface {\r\n\treadonly instructions: InstructionManagerInterface\r\n\treadonly documents: DocumentManagerInterface\r\n\treadonly tools: ToolManagerInterface\r\n}\r\n```\r\n\r\nCall `context.instructions.add(input)`, not `context.addInstruction(input)`.\r\n\r\n### Split behavioral variants\r\n\r\nDo not hide multiple algorithms behind a discriminator parameter:\r\n\r\n```ts\r\nparseJSON(input)\r\nparseYAML(input)\r\nparseTOML(input)\r\n```\r\n\r\nDo not write `parse(input, format)`. A literal that selects a different action is a magic mode and requires separate functions/methods.\r\n\r\nDistinguish behavior from data:\r\n\r\n- Different value selects a different action/algorithm/shape of work → split.\r\n- Different value selects a datum for the same operation → keep it as data.\r\n- Discriminants, reasoning/error codes, uniformly applied value enums, and field/key selectors are data.\r\n\r\n## Standalone helpers\r\n\r\nModule helpers have no owning entity at the call site, so default to `{verb}{Noun}`:\r\n\r\n`generateId`, `computeHash`, `extractColumns`, `derivePhaseStatus`, `inferLanguage`, `normalizePath`, `sanitizeFilename`, `matchesGlobPattern`, `belongsTo`, `hasMany`.\r\n\r\n- A one-word helper is valid only when its meaning and arguments are unmistakable: `delay`, `clamp`, `tokenize`, `similarity`.\r\n- Reject vague helpers such as `process` or `handle`.\r\n- A helper prefix has one project-wide meaning:\r\n  - `extract*` extracts structure.\r\n  - `infer*` derives.\r\n  - `compute*` calculates deterministically.\r\n  - `matches*` is a predicate.\r\n  - `build*` assembles a composite value from parts and is neither a factory nor a combinator named for its constituents; see `create*` and `*Of` in § Fixed derivation/construction forms.\r\n  - `read*` obtains a value from a live host object, a stream position, or a byte layout, returns it or throws, and never coerces; a coercing helper is `parse*` in § Fixed derivation/construction forms.\r\n  - `resolve*` picks the effective value from options and defaults.\r\n  - `scan*` walks a structure and returns its findings.\r\n  - `describe*` takes a finding and returns the human-readable message that names it.\r\n  - `normalize*` returns the canonical form of a value of the same type.\r\n  - `collect*` gathers members into a collection.\r\n  - `filter*` returns the members of a collection that satisfy a predicate, in order, and never mutates its input.\r\n  - `render*` produces text or markup from a value that is not a finding.\r\n  - `supports*` is a capability predicate and narrows no type.\r\n- When a helper family grows around one shape, promote it to a class with entity-scoped one-word methods.\r\n\r\n## General vocabulary\r\n\r\nThe root design laws in `AGENTS.md` — one term per concept, boolean behavior switches, `undefined` absence, derived state, real domain states, and named discriminants — bind here. This section adds only their naming specifics:\r\n\r\n- Describe what a thing is, not its implementation.\r\n- Prefer short common English; avoid jargon, abbreviations, and non-universal acronyms.\r\n- Properties are nouns; methods are verbs.\r\n- Booleans read as assertions: `aborted`, `exhausted`, `expired`.\r\n- Accessors use bare nouns, never `get*`/`set*`.\r\n- Do not alternate `count`/`length`/`size`/`total` or `abort`/`cancel`.\r\n- Name the axis a discriminant varies: `relationship`, `command`, `category`, `operation`, `via`.\r\n- A binary switch is a boolean such as `bail`, never `'continue' | 'halt'`; genuine discriminants, multi-state lifecycles, conventional value pairs (`ascending`/`descending`, `and`/`or`), and external-spec literals remain unions.\r\n- An option key, constant, or member that transliterates an external protocol field, format field, or engine pragma keeps the external wording in this project's casing, and its TSDoc names the source it mirrors: the `foreignKeys` key mirrors the `PRAGMA foreign_keys` statement, and the `keepAlive` key mirrors the Ollama `keep_alive` field.\r\n- Outside a declared wire body, a mirrored name never uses `kind` or `type` as a member name, and never uses a word § Rejected naming lists. A Compound File Binary (CFB) directory entry's object-type byte takes a named discriminant.\r\n- A declared wire body — a type whose members transliterate an external wire format field for field — keeps the external field names, `type` and `kind` included, and its TSDoc names the format it transliterates. The package's own domain type carries neither word, and the package owns the projection between the wire body and the domain type.\r\n\r\n## Acronyms\r\n\r\nKeep canonical case:\r\n\r\n- Initialisms: `JSON`, `HTTP`, `NDJSON`, `SSE`, `URL`, `URI`, `SQL`, `API`, `DB`, `TTL`, `UUID`, `RFC`, `JWT`.\r\n- Brands/proper names: `SQLite`, `IndexedDB`, `GitHub`, `OAuth`.\r\n- PascalCase identifiers preserve the acronym: `JSONSchema`, `HTTPServer`, `NDJSONParser`, `SSEStream`, `URLPattern`, `SQLiteDriver`, `IndexedDBDriver`.\r\n- camelCase lowers an acronym only when it leads: `jsonValue`, `urlPath`, `sqlText`; otherwise preserve it: `parseJSON`, `toJSON`, `fromNDJSON`, `signJWT`, `compileSQL`.\r\n- Ecosystem-conventional `id` and leading `url`/`uri` remain lowercase.\r\n- Domain folders remain lowercase: `http/`, `sqlite/`, `indexeddb/`, `websocket/`.\r\n- Never title-fold canonical acronyms into `Json`, `Ndjson`, `Http`, `Sse`, or `Sqlite`.\r\n\r\n## Type-level identifiers\r\n\r\n| Kind                      | Required form                           |\r\n| ------------------------- | --------------------------------------- |\r\n| Behavioral interface      | `{Entity}Interface` `{Entity}Result`                        |\r\n| Execution context         | `{Entity}Context`                       |\r\n| Event map                 | `{Entity}EventMap`                      |\r\n| Union/enum-like           | `{Entity}{Noun}`                        |\r\n| Plain non-behavioral data | `{Entity}`                              |\r\n| Function type             | `{Entity}Handler` or `{Entity}Function` |\r\n| Manager interface         | `{Entity}ManagerInterface`              |\r\n\r\n- Never prefix interfaces with `I`.\r\n- Never pluralize type names.\r\n- `Handler` is for function types, never classes.\r\n\r\n## Value-level identifiers\r\n\r\n| Kind           | Required form                         |\r\n| -------------- | ------------------------------------- |\r\n| Class          | PascalCase `{Entity}`                 |\r\n| Manager class  | PascalCase `{Entity}Manager`          |\r\n| Factory        | camelCase `create{Entity}`            |\r\n| Guard          | camelCase `is{Condition}`             |\r\n| Helper         | camelCase `{verb}{Noun}`              |\r\n| Constant       | UPPER_SNAKE_CASE `{QUALIFIER}_{NOUN}` |\r\n| Property/field | camelCase bare noun                   |\r\n| Method         | camelCase bare verb                   |\r\n| Boolean        | camelCase adjective/past participle   |\r\n\r\n## Fixed derivation/construction forms\r\n\r\n- A form's contract binds a new name; `.claude/rules/architecture.md` § Kind purity names the retained names that keep a form outside its file, such as `createWriteDirectory` and `isVacant`.\r\n- `is*`: total `Guard<T>`; never throws; returns false off-shape.\r\n- `parse*`: coercion producing `T | undefined`; cross-type conversion never belongs in a guard.\r\n- `create*`: the factory form; `.claude/rules/architecture.md` § Kind purity states what a factory is and where it lives.\r\n- `*Of`: combinator named for its constituents, combining them into a container/guard/value, such as `arrayOf(guard)` or `boundsOf(min, max)`.\r\n- `{noun}To{Noun}`: projection from a whole to a derived view, such as `definitionToSnapshot`.\r\n- `*Shape`: `ContractShape` value/JSON-Schema coexist, name each fact: a pool may expose `size`, `idle`, and `active`; a queue may expose `count` and `active`.\r\n- Do not expose several ambiguous `count` properties.\r\n\r\n## Files and
