The root completion law decides what may not be left behind.\r\n6. **Prove each defect before repairing it.** A repair begins with a test that fails for that defect: record the exact command and its failing count before the fix and the same command's passing count after. A repair with no red-then-green record is unproven.\r\n7. **Consolidate.** Run the complete centralization and wrapper sweep. When the claim is that a foreign client can use this package, drive one representative real client end to end.\r\n10. **Document the final behavior.** Update the governing guide, examples, method tables, limitations, and parity coverage. Add a dedicated adversarial round for security, concurrency, destructive paths, or external input. A unit's auditor is an engine that did not write it; same-engine re-review returns the author's own blind spot. Resolve every required finding, then rerun affected verification.\r\n\r\n## Accept the result\r\n\r\nDo not accept “mostly complete,” “tests pass” without adequacy review, or a builder's self-report. Completion requires:\r\n\r\n- all requested capabilities implemented or explicitly proven out of scope;\r\n- red-then-green evidence for every repaired defect;\r\n- public contract, implementation, tests, guides, and generated outputs aligned;\r\n- no unresolved centralization, wrapper, dependency-reuse, test-discovery, lifecycle, security, or text-integrity finding;\r\n- exact command evidence for every final gate.\r\n\r\nCompletion is those conditions met, not the absence of anything further to find. When every row is closed and the gates are green, stopping is the correct action and the next campaign is the deliverable; a further pass over the same surface is a new instruction from the user, not a finding.\r\n\r\nReport what changed, what evidence proves it, exact test/gate results, and any genuine residual risk. Do not call an in-scope omission “future work.”\r\n# Adopt `@orkestrel/contract`\r\n\r\n## Inspect the exact installed package\r\n\r\nNever work from a remembered contract version or copied API list. Read:\r\n\r\n1. `package.json` and the lockfile for the declared and resolved versions.\r\n2. The vendored `guides/contract.md` when present.\r\n3. The installed package's declaration file and exports.\r\n4. Relevant package source or current canonical guide when semantics remain unclear.\r\n\r\nDo not add `@orkestrel/contract` unless the user authorizes a dependency change. When it is already declared, use it to its full semantically appropriate extent.\r\n\r\n## Build an overlap matrix\r\n\r\nInventory local code involving:\r\n\r\n- primitive, structural, instance, collection, and total guards;\r\n- guard combinators and refinements;\r\n- coercing parsers and field readers;\r\n- JSON parsing and narrowing;\r\n- `Result`, success/failure outcomes, and safe exception capture;\r\n- contract shapes, compiled guards/parsers/schemas/generators/reporters;\r\n- finite/integer checks, error narrowing, and hostile-boundary containment.\r\n\r\nFor each local symbol, record:\r\n\r\n| Local symbol | Contract candidate | Same semantics? | Difference | Decision | Required tests |\r\n| ------------ | ------------------ | --------------- | ---------- | -------- | -------------- |\r\n\r\nReplace only when semantics match or the current contract primitive is intentionally better for the desired API. A similar name is not proof.\r\n\r\n## Refactor without wrappers\r\n\r\n- Import the originating contract symbol directly when it already expresses the required operation.\r\n- Update downstream call sites atomically.\r\n- Delete rename-only, pass-through, and compatibility wrappers.\r\n- Keep a local helper only when it adds a real invariant, composition, projection, domain translation, or boundary.\r\n- Do not re-export contract symbols from another package's barrel.\r\n- Prefer one compiled contract over independently maintained guard/parser/schema logic when the package genuinely needs those outputs.\r\n- Prefer contract outcomes and safe attempt boundaries over repeated ad hoc `try`/`catch` shapes when their failure semantics match.\r\n\r\nDo not force contract abstractions into plain control flow where they obscure behavior or change the error contract.\r\n\r\n## Prove strictness and soundness\r\n\r\nTest every adopted boundary for relevant invariants:\r\n\r\n- guards are total for adversarial values and hostile prototypes;\r\n- parsed values satisfy the paired guard;\r\n- guard-valid values retain identity when promised;\r\n- invalid or ambiguous values fail in the documented form;\r\n- caught non-`Error` throws normalize as documented;\r\n- compiled schema, parser, guard, generator, and reporter stay aligned when used;\r\n- finite, integer, `NaN`, infinities, signed zero, cycles, depth, and cross-realm behavior are covered where applicable.\r\n\r\nRun the centralization sweep after replacement so obsolete local types, helpers, constants, and tests do not remain.\r\n# Centralization and simplification\r\n\r\nPlacement, kind purity, the wrapper test, the no-nested-function law, and barrel law live\r\nin `.claude/rules/architecture.md`; shared test infrastructure and helper placement live in\r\n`.claude/rules/tests.md`. This reference adds only the sweep those laws assume: what to\r\ninventory, how to classify, and what must be proven before acceptance.\r\n\r\n## Inventory what you touched\r\n\r\nInspect every touched implementation and centralized file, then sweep the full affected\r\nenvironments.\r\n\r\n| Where               | Inventory                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |\r\n| ------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |\r\n| Implementation file | Interfaces and type aliases, module constants and data, free functions/guards/parsers/factories/schemas, function declarations or assignments inside bodies, extra classes, imports and exports stranded by a move                                            |\r\n| Centralized file    | Every module declaration: does it match that file's kind, is it exported, is every intentional top-level export reachable from the correct environment barrel regardless of current consumers, does it carry direct behavioral coverage when it carries logic |\r\n| Environment root    | Every declaration promoted there: at least two consuming modules, or it belongs to the owning module's centralized file                                                                                                                                       |\r\n\r\nThe rare runtime-self-contained entrypoint exception must be literally required for\r\nexecution and must explain why sibling imports cannot work.\r\n\r\n## Classify every function\r\n\r\n| Signal                                     | Home                                                 |\r\n| ------------------------------------------ | ---------------------------------------------------- |\r\n| Reaches instance state or a sibling method | Class method                                         |\r\n| Pure self-contained computation            | Exported centralized helper, parser, compiler, guard |\r\n| Defining recursive or compositional spine  | Class method, after extracting its pure leaves       |\r\n| Trivial and genuinely one-use              | Inline it into the caller                            |\r\n\r\nNever move logic into a nested function to evade centralization. An anonymous callback\r\npassed directly to another operation stays a callback, not a hidden helper declaration.\r\n\r\n## Hunt the wrapper\r\n\r\nSearch callers and callees for one-line delegates, pass-through factories, getters that\r\nrename another public getter, duplicate guards or parsers a declared dependency already\r\nsupplies, compatibility aliases and re-exports, and functions whose only purpose is\r\navoiding a downstream rename. The architecture rules' wrapper test decides each one; a\r\nsurvivor owns a real boundary, invariant, composition, translation, lifecycle, or\r\nmaterially narrower contract. Otherwise use or rename the real symbol and update every\r\nconsumer.\r\n\r\nDo not hollow a class into public methods that each forward to one helper.\r\n\r\n## Consolidate tests\r\n\r\nSweep test files for repeated or reusable input and result records, builders and\r\nfactories, recorders and event capture, wait and readiness helpers, temporary workspace or\r\nfixture-server setup, browser and DOM builders, event factories, and service request\r\nbuilders or response assertions. Move each into the setup file its environment owns.\r\n\r\nAdd focused tests for every exported function extracted from production code. Do not create\r\nisolated tests for declaration-only types, constants, barrels, or error definitions.\r\n\r\n## Prove the sweep\r\n\r\nBefore acceptance, prove:\r\n\r\n- no stray declarations remain in implementation files;\r\n- no non-exported or wrong-kind declaration remains in centralized files;\r\n- no prohibited nested function declaration or assignment remains;\r\n- no duplicate or rename-only wrapper remains;\r\n- every move updated imports, barrels, guides, and tests;\r\n- test helpers are consolidated without over-generalizing one-off setup;\r\n- files are valid UTF-8 with no replacement characters, mojibake, unintended control\r\n  characters, or accidental trailing debris.\r\n\r\nReview the complete diff after formatting. Formatting cannot substitute for the structural\r\nsweep.\r\nimport type { BudgetInterface, TokenUsage } from '@orkestrel/budget'\r\nimport type { EmitterErrorHandler, EmitterHooks, EmitterInterface } from '@orkestrel/emitter'\r\nimport type { QueueStoreInterface } from '@orkestrel/queue'\r\nimport type {\r\n\tToolCall,\r\n\tToolDefinition,\r\n\tToolInterface,\r\n\tToolManagerInterface,\r\n\tToolResult,\r\n} from '@orkestrel/tool'\r\nimport type { SchedulerInterface } from '@orkestrel/workflow'\r\nimport type { WorkspaceManagerInterface } from '@orkestrel/workspace'\r\n\r\n/** Names the role a {@link Message} plays in a conversation turn. */\r\nexport type MessageRole = 'system' | 'user' | 'assistant' | 'tool'\r\n\r\n/**\r\n * Represents one conversation turn fed to a {@link ProviderInterface} — a stored, identified\r\n * message.\r\n *\r\n * @remarks\r\n * `calls` is present only on an `assistant` turn that requested tool calls — the\r\n * `tool_calls` a prior generation produced, replayed back into the next request so\r\n * the model sees its own decision. A `tool` turn carries the tool's result in\r\n * `content` (the textual outcome), keyed back to the call by the conversation order.\r\n */\r\nexport interface Message {\r\n\treadonly id: string\r\n\treadonly role: MessageRole\r\n\treadonly content: string\r\n\t/** Holds an assistant turn's requested tools — its `tool_calls`, replayed. */\r\n\treadonly calls?: readonly ToolCall[]\r\n\t/**\r\n\t * Holds multimodal image data attached to this turn — base64-encoded image strings,\r\n\t * forwarded to a vision-capable provider (the provider maps them onto the wire's\r\n\t * per-message `images` array). Present only on a multimodal turn; absent otherwise.\r\n\t */\r\n\treadonly images?: readonly string[]\r\n}\r\n\r\n/**\r\n * Carries the minimal data needed to author a {@link Message} — the `id` is\r\n * assigned by the layer that stores it, so a caller supplies only role / content\r\n * (and, for a replayed assistant turn, its `calls`).\r\n */\r\nexport interface MessageInput {\r\n\treadonly role: MessageRole\r\n\treadonly content: string\r\n\treadonly calls?: readonly ToolCall[]\r\n\t/**\r\n\t * Holds multimodal image data for this turn — base64-encoded image strings forwarded to a\r\n\t * vision-capable provider (carried verbatim onto the stored {@link Message}).\r\n\t */\r\n\treadonly images?: readonly string[]\r\n}\r\n\r\n/**\r\n * Holds a single inference turn's structured outcome — the assembled assistant content,\r\n * any reasoning the provider separated from it, any tool calls the model requested,\r\n * and the token usage it reported.\r\n *\r\n * @remarks\r\n * `thinking` is present only when the turn produced reasoning the provider split\r\n * away from the answer (an in-content `<think>…</think>` span a thinking model\r\n * emitted, or a wire-side reasoning field) — `content` is always the clean answer,\r\n * and the thinking never re-enters the conversation (it is display/audit metadata,\r\n * not prompt text). `tools` is present only when the model wants tool calls (an\r\n * empty array is never surfaced — its absence means \"no calls\"). `usage` is present\r\n * only when the wire reported it (on the stream's `done` line, or the non-stream\r\n * body), so a caller folds it into a token budget exactly when it exists.\r\n */\r\nexport interface ProviderResult {\r\n\treadonly content: string\r\n\t/** Carries reasoning the provider separated from the answer when present — never re-enters the conversation. */\r\n\treadonly thinking?: string\r\n\t/** Carries the tool calls the model wants when present. */\r\n\treadonly tools?: readonly ToolCall[]\r\n\t/** Carries token consumption for this turn when present (from the wire's `done` line / body). */\r\n\treadonly usage?: TokenUsage\r\n}\r\n\r\n/**\r\n * Represents one streamed delta a {@link ProviderInterface}'s `stream` yields — a unit tagged by\r\n * the channel it belongs to, so the agent loop can re-surface answer content and live reasoning\r\n * separately as it pumps.\r\n *\r\n * @remarks\r\n * The discriminant `channel` names the axis that varies: a\r\n * `'content'` delta is a chunk of the assistant answer (the deltas that accumulate into\r\n * {@link ProviderResult.content}); a `'thinking'` delta is a chunk of the model's\r\n * reasoning the provider separated from the answer (the daemon's native\r\n * `message.thinking` wire channel), surfaced live so a consumer can stream it into a\r\n * collapsible without waiting for the assembled result. `text` is the delta's literal\r\n * text. Thinking never re-enters the conversation — it is display/audit metadata, exactly\r\n * as {@link ProviderResult.thinking} (the authoritative final accumulation) is.\r\n */\r\nexport type ProviderDelta =\r\n\t| { readonly channel: 'content'; readonly text: string }\r\n\t| { readonly channel: 'thinking'; readonly text: string }\r\n\r\n/**\r\n * Carries the per-call options threaded into a {@link ProviderInterface}'s `generate` / `stream` —\r\n * the bag a caller passes to influence one inference call without reconfiguring the provider\r\n * instance.\r\n *\r\n * @remarks\r\n * `think` overrides the provider's constructed reasoning preference for this call: `true`\r\n * asks the backend to separate reasoning natively (a thinking model returns it on its\r\n * `message.thinking` channel, surfaced as `'thinking'` {@link ProviderDelta}s + the final\r\n * {@link ProviderResult.thinking}); `false` suppresses it. `schema`, when given, asks the\r\n * backend to constrain its response to the given JSON-Schema shape (the same open\r\n * JSON-Schema record {@link ToolDefinition.parameters} already carries) — a structured-output\r\n * request for this call only. Both omitted ⇒ the provider's own defaults apply (the\r\n * constructor value / no schema constraint), so the contract stays backward-safe — a caller\r\n * that passes no options behaves exactly as before.\r\n */\r\nexport interface ProviderStreamOptions {\r\n\t/** Overrides the provider's reasoning preference for this call; omitted ⇒ the provider default. */\r\n\treadonly think?: boolean\r\n\t/** Constrains the response to this JSON-Schema shape (the same open record {@link ToolDefinition.parameters} uses); omitted ⇒ no constraint. */\r\n\treadonly schema?: Readonly<Record<string, unknown>>\r\n}\r\n\r\n/**\r\n * Defines the pluggable LLM inference boundary — the one contract every agent chunk depends on. A\r\n * provider turns a conversation (plus optional tools) into either a single assembled {@link\r\n * ProviderResult} (`generate`) or a stream of {@link ProviderDelta}s that returns the assembled\r\n * result (`stream`).\r\n *\r\n * @remarks\r\n * - `id` is a stable per-instance trace label; `name` identifies the backend\r\n *   (`'ollama'`).\r\n * - Both calls take an `AbortSignal` so a caller bounds the request (cancel,\r\n *   deadline, or budget folded through `AbortSignal.any`); aborting a `stream` mid-flight\r\n *   surfaces a `ProviderAbortError` carrying the partial result.\r\n * - `tools`, when given non-empty, advertises the callable tools for this turn.\r\n * - `options` carries the optional per-call {@link ProviderStreamOptions} (for example `think`),\r\n *   overriding the provider's constructed defaults for that one call; omitted ⇒ defaults.\r\n */\r\nexport interface ProviderInterface {\r\n\treadonly id: string\r\n\treadonly name: string\r\n\t/**\r\n\t * Holds the model's preferred context framing, by section kind — an optional\r\n\t * {@link ContextFormat} an {@link import('./AgentContext.js').AgentContext}\r\n\t * applies as the provider-default level of its build cascade (beating the managers'\r\n\t * built-in framing, beaten by a manager-options or per-item override). Omitted ⇒ the\r\n\t * provider is framing-agnostic and the managers' built-in defaults apply unchanged.\r\n\t */\r\n\treadonly format?: ContextFormat | undefined\r\n\t/**\r\n\t * Generates one complete turn — resolves the assembled {@link ProviderResult}.\r\n\t *\r\n\t * @param messages - The conversation so far\r\n\t * @param signal - Bounds the request; an abort rejects the call\r\n\t * @param tools - Optional tools the model may call this turn\r\n\t * @param options - Optional per-call {@link ProviderStreamOptions} (for example `think`); omitted ⇒ defaults\r\n\t * @returns The assembled result (content + any tool calls + any usage)\r\n\t */\r\n\tgenerate(\r\n\t\tmessages: readonly Message[],\r\n\t\tsignal: AbortSignal,\r\n\t\ttools?: readonly ToolDefinition[],\r\n\t\toptions?: ProviderStreamOptions,\r\n\t): Promise<ProviderResult>\r\n\t/**\r\n\t * Streams one turn — yields channel-tagged `content` / `thinking` {@link ProviderDelta}s as\r\n\t * they arrive and returns the assembled {@link ProviderResult} (the concatenated content,\r\n\t * any separated reasoning, any tool calls, and any usage) when the stream completes. A\r\n\t * mid-stream abort throws a `ProviderAbortError` carrying the partial result.\r\n\t *\r\n\t * @remarks\r\n\t * The `partial` holds whatever streamed before the cancel, so a caller can recover the\r\n\t * partial content.\r\n\t *\r\n\t * @param messages - The conversation so far\r\n\t * @param signal - Bounds the request; an abort throws `ProviderAbortError`\r\n\t * @param tools - Optional tools the model may call this turn\r\n\t * @param options - Optional per-call {@link ProviderStreamOptions} (for example `think`); omitted ⇒ defaults\r\n\t * @returns A generator of {@link ProviderDelta}s, returning the assembled result\r\n\t */\r\n\tstream(\r\n\t\tmessages: readonly Message[],\r\n\t\tsignal: AbortSignal,\r\n\t\ttools?: readonly ToolDefinition[],\r\n\t\toptions?: ProviderStreamOptions,\r\n\t): AsyncGenerator<ProviderDelta, ProviderResult>\r\n}\r\n\r\n/**\r\n * Splits a thinking model's in-content `<think>…</think>` reasoning spans away from the answer,\r\n * delta by delta with per-stream state, so a provider yields clean content alone and surfaces the\r\n * reasoning as {@link ProviderResult.thinking}.\r\n *\r\n * @remarks\r\n * - **Stateful across deltas.** A tag may arrive split across wire chunks (`'<thi'`\r\n *   ending one delta, `'nk>'` opening the next) — `split` holds any ambiguous tail\r\n *   back until the next delta (or `flush`) disambiguates it, so a partial tag is\r\n *   never leaked as content and never mis-eaten as thinking.\r\n * - **`split(delta)`** feeds one raw content delta and returns the clean content to\r\n *   surface for it (possibly `''` — for example mid-think). Text inside a\r\n *   `<think>…</think>` span accumulates on `thinking`; multiple spans accumulate in\r\n *   order; a nested-looking `<think>` inside an open span is thinking text (no\r\n *   nesting — the first `</think>` closes).\r\n * - **The implicit leading open (the qwen3-template shape).** Some chat templates\r\n *   pre-seed `<think>` into the prompt scaffold, so the wire stream begins\r\n *   mid-reasoning and only a bare `</think>` ever appears. Before any tag event, a\r\n *   bare close therefore reclassifies everything surfaced so far (plus the pre-close\r\n *   pending) as thinking — `content` is corrected retroactively, while the already\r\n *   `split`-returned prefix cannot be recalled (the one shape where the per-delta\r\n *   returns over-report; `content` stays authoritative). The rule is one-shot: after\r\n *   any tag event a bare `</think>` is plain text (prose quoting the tag stays text).\r\n * - **`flush()`** settles the stream end: an unclosed `<think>` tail (the model was\r\n *   cut off mid-reasoning) lands on `thinking`; a held partial tag that never\r\n *   completed (`'<thi'` then EOF) is returned as the final clean-content delta —\r\n *   it was real text after all.\r\n * - **`content` / `thinking`** are the authoritative accumulations so far (read them\r\n *   after the stream — or mid-stream for a cancel's partial); `content` is the one\r\n *   exact clean-content source (the per-delta returns match it except across an\r\n *   implicit-open reclassification). One splitter serves one stream; create a fresh\r\n *   one per call ({@link import('./factories.js').createThinkSplitter}).\r\n */\r\nexport interface ThinkSplitterInterface {\r\n\t/** Holds the authoritative clean content accumulated so far (corrected across an implicit-open reclassification). */\r\n\treadonly content: string\r\n\t/** Holds the reasoning text accumulated from every `<think>…</think>` span so far. */\r\n\treadonly thinking: string\r\n\t/**\r\n\t * Feeds one raw delta and returns the clean, non-think content to surface for it (possibly\r\n\t * `''`) — a tag split across deltas is held until disambiguated, never leaked as content\r\n\t * and never mis-eaten as thinking.\r\n\t */\r\n\tsplit(delta: string): string\r\n\t/**\r\n\t * Settles the stream end — a held partial tag that never completed returns as the final\r\n\t * content delta, and an unclosed think span's tail lands on `thinking`.\r\n\t */\r\n\tflush(): string\r\n}\r\n\r\n/**\r\n * Stores immutable {@link Message}s in insertion order and mints each `id` on `add` — the\r\n * message-store contract {@link AgentContextInterface.messages} is typed to, which the active\r\n * {@link ConversationInterface} satisfies structurally.\r\n *\r\n * @remarks\r\n * - **Store.** Messages live in insertion order; `count` is how many are stored.\r\n *   `add` takes one {@link MessageInput} or a batch and mints each message's\r\n *   `id` (a random UUID), returning the created message(s). A stored message is\r\n *   immutable — created once from its input, never mutated.\r\n * - **Lookup.** `message(id)` resolves one by id (`undefined` when absent);\r\n *   `messages()` lists every message in insertion order.\r\n * - **Removal.** `remove` drops one by id, or a batch — `true` only when every supplied id\r\n *   was removed; `clear` empties the store.\r\n * - **Event-free.** A purely data store — no Emitter, no events.\r\n */\r\nexport interface MessageManagerInterface {\r\n\treadonly count: number\r\n\t/**\r\n\t * Stores one {@link MessageInput}, or a batch — mints each Separate verified fact from inference.\r\n- Read authoritative types and named decision-bearing implementation files first-hand. Delegate bulk supporting context, never the owning design decision.\r\n- Treat existing code, tests, `old/`, branches, and copied projects as evidence, not authority.\r\n- Build a capability/defect matrix before a broad API or production-readiness change. Every row ends as implement, repair, retain, or intentionally exclude with evidence.\r\n- Fix that matrix when the change starts. It is the definition of done, and the change closes when every row closes.\r\n- Never end a row as \"hardened further.\" Replace any evaluative phrase with the concrete condition that closes the row.\r\n- Record a finding outside the matrix against the row that owns it, for the next matrix. Do not reopen this one.\r\n\r\n## Probes before arguments\r\n\r\nA question a probe can settle is settled by the probe, whether or not anyone has disputed it. Reasoning decides what to run and what the result means. It does not replace the run.\r\n\r\n- Test your own assumptions before you rely on them. You do not need a disagreement to justify a probe. If you are about to depend on what a function returns, what a config resolves to, what a flag does, or whether a path is even reached, run it and find out.\r\n- Treat a long deliberation about behaviour as the signal to stop and run something. Deliberation that a ten-line probe would have ended is the most expensive habit in this process, and it is invisible because it feels like rigour.\r\n- Verify a belief before stating it, or label it as unverified. An unverified assertion put into context becomes a fact for everything downstream, including other agents, and correcting it later costs more than the check would have.\r\n- Hold a claim you issue to the standard you hold a claim you receive. Nothing downstream re-checks an instruction, so an unverified fact inside one becomes the premise of every task built on it. Auditing every report you receive while exempting every brief you write is the common form of this failure.\r\n- Prefer the check over the chain of reasoning whenever the check is cheap. Reading the installed declaration, running one line, or counting the call sites beats three paragraphs inferring the same thing, and it produces evidence you can hand to someone else.\r\n- Write the probe before the argument gets long. The moment a claim about behaviour is disputed — between engines, between a report and a rule, or in your own head — stop and run it. Get the smallest real input through the real code and read the real output.\r\n- Bound the search before starting it, and put the bound in the brief. Name the benchmark, the population, or the row that ends it. An investigation with no stated stopping condition runs until attention runs out. This includes the case where the honest answer is that the limit is inherent and belongs in documentation rather than in code.\r\n- Treat a negative probe as evidence about the probe until its input is shown to reach the code under test. A pass proves nothing if the vector never arrived. Instrument the path, assert an observable side effect, or drive it through a door you can see.\r\n- When a report names a defect and your reproduction comes back clean, assume first that your vector was weaker than theirs, and go get their exact vector. Treating a failed reproduction as a disproof is the most common way a true finding is lost.\r\n- A reported vector the compiler rejects refutes the VECTOR, never the finding. Where a claim asserts reachability through a typed API, compile the exact vector under the project's own settings; re-derive one the types admit before dropping the finding, and record which vector was actually tested.\r\n- Reproduce a reported defect's cause before instructing a fix from it. A real symptom can carry a wrong diagnosis, and a fix aimed at the stated cause edits the wrong file while the defect survives.\r\n- Do not read a result into a tool's failure to find your probe. \"No tests found\", an empty match, a skipped file, a runner that resolved nothing — these report on the harness, not the subject. Confirm the probe was collected and executed first.\r\n- Prefer an observation over a derivation, including your own. When a measurement and an argument disagree, the argument is wrong until the measurement is shown to be broken.\r\n- Diagnose from the artifact the work produces — the file, the count, the exit code, the timestamp on what changed — never from a wrapper or a proxy signal that merely correlates with progress. Read a gate bare: a pipeline stage after it (`| tail`, `| grep`) reports the stage's exit status and hides the gate's failing lines.\r\n- Verify a comment or an agent's report against the call sites before relying on it. A code comment is not evidence. When lanes disagree about whether a path is live, count the callers rather than weighing the prose.\r\n- What a round proves is what it ran. A conclusion carried from one door to another is a hypothesis at the second door. Re-run it there.\r\n\r\n## Falsification\r\n\r\nA review that reads a diff finds what the diff shows. A review that tries to break named claims finds what the diff hides. Code that has passed diff review several times can still carry a defect nobody has tried to trigger.\r\n\r\n### Writing the claims\r\n\r\n- State the subject as a numbered list of the claims the work makes, never as \"review this diff.\" Each claim is falsifiable: a property some concrete input, state, or interleaving could show false.\r\n- Instruct the auditor to attempt refutation, not confirmation. A claim it cannot break is CONFIRMED with the evidence that convinced it. A claim it breaks is BROKEN with the exact failing input, state, or interleaving, plus the smallest correct fix.\r\n- Derive claims from what the change asserts under adverse conditions: cancellation, restart, concurrency, partial failure, hostile input, resource exhaustion, and the orderings a happy path never reaches.\r\n- Read the installed declaration or implementation of every substrate a claim depends on. A claim about `stop()` is unfalsifiable until you know what `stop()` does when the status is not the one the caller assumed.\r\n\r\n### Instruments\r\n\r\n- An instrument is not evidence until it has failed. Pair every probe, comparison, or matrix with a negative control that must report failure, run under the same conditions. An identity check whose control reports \"same\" has measured nothing.\r\n- When a question about a TypeScript edit can supply a workspace project, a case of workspace files with a test, and a negative control naming its files, its test, the stage it must fail at, and why, call the `prove` tool the `probe` MCP server registers before relying on the answer. When the question supplies no project, no case, or no control, follow `.claude/rules/tests.md` § Probes and report the fallback instrument's own control and coverage.\r\n- When no `probe` server is registered in the session, register one outside the repository, in the harness's own local or user MCP scope — in Claude Code, `claude mcp add` outside project scope — naming the installed `node_modules/@orkestrel/probe/dist/bin/main.js` entry, and start it in the repository whose projects the question names, because the server fixes its workspace from its own working directory. The registration cannot live in the tree: a scaffold target holds no `.mcp.json` file, that path is instruction canon, and a copy at it reports as foreign drift on every `scaffold audit` run. Where the harness registers no server at all, treat the question as supplying no project and take the preceding rule's fallback.\r\n- Quote the closing line of the `prove` answer verbatim in every report, brief, and audit verdict that rests on the claim: the `receipt probe:<digest>:…` line when the case ran clean and the control broke exactly where the claim declared it would, and the `no receipt` line otherwise. A `no receipt` line leaves the claim unproved — report it with the stage that refused.\r\n- Read a receipt as evidence about its claim, never as a gate result. The gate chain still runs, and `verifier` still owns its result.\r\n- Draw the negative control from outside the population the instrument covers. Name the instrument's membership rule first, then pick a control that rule excludes. A control sampled from constructs the instrument already handles proves only that it discriminates among those constructs, and says nothing about the class it silently cannot reach.\r\n- State an instrument's coverage beside its result. A conclusion inherits the instrument's scope, not the question's. An unstated coverage claim is read as complete, and it never is. A search proves something about the paths it walked, so name them.\r\n- Match the instrument to the question. A text search reports on text, so a claim about declarations, call sites, or structure needs the compiler or a parser instead. A pattern written for one spelling of a construct reports on that spelling alone. A path check answers relative to the directory it runs from, so resolve the inputs against their own base before reading a miss as a finding.\r\n- Name the rival reading the instrument must exclude, and show it reports differently under that reading. Give independent measurements independent state: one counter shared across members reports read order and per-member read count identically, so a result consistent with both measured neither.\r\n- Report a question unanswered rather than answering it with a weaker instrument. A fallback that measures something adjacent returns a confident wrong answer, and nothing downstream can tell that answer from the real one — searching commit messages for a release when the question is where a version changed will match some release, but not the one asked about. Name the substitute and what it actually measures, or say the question is open.\r\n- State what the controls established and what they did not. An instrument certified only from the inside is trusted exactly where it has never been tested.\r\n- Treat a gap between what an instrument says it checks and what it actually matches as a defect in the instrument, not as a documented limit. A recorded blind spot buys trust only when everything outside it is genuinely covered.\r\n- Measure the product, not the harness. A recorded baseline that counts something about its own fixture is not evidence about the shipped surface, however often a guide quotes it.\r\n- Baseline a published-artifact claim against the published artifact. \"Did my change move the surface\" and \"does this release differ from the last one\" are different questions, and a diff against your own starting point answers only the first. A toolchain that re-emits declarations moves the artifact without any source edit, so every writer can correctly report an unmoved surface while the package's published contract has changed. Fetch what consumers actually have — the tarball, the deployed asset — and compare against that.\r\n- Prove a module cycle by loading the built artifact, not by a green suite. Tests import through the source graph and a bundler resolves it differently, so a cycle that is fatal at module-init in the shipped form can stay invisible under every test. Import each published entry point and read an export from it.\r\n- Adopt an instrument that settled a claim as a test before accepting the work it settled. The probe that proved a fix, carrying the control that proved the probe, is that fix's regression guard. A verification that runs once is a rehearsal, not a gate.\r\n\r\n### Rounds and verdicts\r\n\r\n- Treat an all-confirmed round as a legitimate result, and put the brief on trial rather than the subject. Re-read the claims and ask whether any could have been falsified by evidence the round actually had. If none could, the claims were descriptive and the round proved nothing — sharpen them and re-run. If they could have been and were not, the pass stands.\r\n- Name the claims you could not break either way, so the next round knows what has already been attacked.\r\n- Never tell an auditor that a clean round means it did not try. That instructs it to manufacture a finding, and a manufactured finding costs a fix unit, an argument, and the credibility of the true findings beside it.\r\n- Treat a repaired claim as a new claim, not a settled one. Re-ask it at every entry point that reaches the same rule, not only the door the defect arrived through. The engine that wrote the fix is least able to see this, because re-verifying where the fix is feels like verifying the fix.\r\n- A fix that adopts the auditor's prescription verbatim may close with a mutation probe in place of a fresh audit round: disable the load-bearing line, watch the adopted pin fail, restore it, and commit the pin as the regression guard. A fix that departs from the prescription gets the cross-engine round.\r\n- Let reachability bound the fix. A defect reachable through the package's own shipped code or a documented extension seam falsifies its claim and is repaired now.\r\n- Document the obligation instead when a defect is reachable only through a hypothetical foreign implementation of a contract this package publishes. State it on the interface that owns it and prove the documentation. Do not build coordination machinery against a requirement nobody wrote down. Attacks are unlimited; reachable ones are not, and only the reachable set is a work list.\r\n- **Three rounds at one seam is the budget, and reaching it switches the search strategy rather than stopping the work.** Repeated rounds against one seam are evidence about the design, not evidence of diligence. At the third round, name what the audit is trying to accomplish, then pick the successor strategy from what the recurrence shows.\r\n- Recurrence with a direction — each fix relocates the class along one stream, or the evidence points up a dependency chain — ends the depth search. Run one breadth round that probes the stream's stations in parallel to locate the source, in the shape `.agents/orchestration.md` § Context and decomposition prescribes, then plan the downstream work from the source and size it by the sweep's map.\r\n- Recurrence with no direction makes the seam itself the question. The next unit is a ruling — on the threat model, the mechanism, or the boundary — taken with the same adversarial pass a design gets, not a fourth repair.\r\n- A subject that reprices itself on every edit — a count, a census, a total over prose — has no closing condition and is not a seam. Drop the claim, or recast it as the property the tally stood in for.\r\n- Write the round count down in the capability/defect matrix row that owns the seam, when the seam opens, so it is a fact rather than a feeling. A seam that has consumed more rounds than the rest of the matrix combined has already answered the question.\r\n- State the ruling that ends a seam as the invariant the code will obey, the constraint bounding it against over-correction, and the interface where a consumer meets the obligation. A ruling that names only the defect it replaces produces the opposite defect next round.\r\n- Give every behavioural audit the means to run its attacks. An auditor that cannot execute cannot falsify a behavioural claim: it returns derivations, and a derivation reads exactly like a verdict while being a different thing — it will confirm a claim one probe would break. Treat a report with no executed evidence as a review of the source, and label it as such.\r\n\r\n## Ecosystem reuse\r\n\r\nThe root laws on inspecting declared `@orkestrel/*` capabilities, reusing a matching primitive, and updating every consumer without shims bind here without restatement. This file adds the judgment calls:\r\n\r\n- Prove the semantic difference before keeping a local variant. Similar names are not evidence of different behavior.\r\n- Treat downstream friction as valid evidence of a reusable upstream defect, not as automatic proof. Fix the lowest package that owns the general mechanism and keep product policy downstream.\r\n- Never re-export a dependency's symbol to soften a consumer's import.\r\n\r\n## Production hardening\r\n\r\n- Translate \"enterprise-grade\" or \"production-ready\" into an explicit risk and seam matrix covering applicable inputs, states, failures, cleanup, cancellation, concurrency, resource ownership, hostile boundaries, environment isolation, serialization and restore, and package consumption.\r\n- Grade that matrix on coverage, not optimization: whether every applicable seam exists, works, and stays proven. Do not grade on how many further interleavings can be invented against the one seam already proven. A surface polished past its row buys less than the next uncovered seam would have.\r\n- Test observable invariants at each applicable seam with real implementations.\r\n- Use dedicated real-service projects for external model or service behavior. Require readiness and tune each request to the smallest proof that still exercises the claim.\r\n- Audit test discovery, counts, skipped and todo tests, cleanup, and assertion adequacy. Passing discovered tests alone is insufficient.\r\n- Inspect public exports, declarations, supported runtime targets, and generated outputs.\r\n- Treat a claim that a surface works with an external client as unproven until one representative real client of that class has driven it end to end. Protocol tests prove the protocol, not the integration.\r\n- Add an independent adversarial review for security, destructive paths, concurrency, protocols, or untrusted external input.\r\n\r\n## Completion\r\n\r\nThe root completion law — finish every in-scope capability now, leave no TODO, deferral, or hidden follow-up, and run the applicable repository skill for comprehensive work — binds here without restatement. This file adds these obligations:\r\n\r\n- Perform a final centralization, wrapper, test-helper, and text-integrity sweep after implementation and before gates.\r\n- Produce local quality gates and relevant output inspection as required evidence.\r\n- Stop once the enumerated scope is closed and those gates are green. Stopping there is correct, and the next scope is the deliverable. A further pass over the same surface requires a new instruction from the user, not an auditor's remaining appetite. Depth is owed to what the scope names, not to what an engine can still imagine about it.\r\n# Developer-facing writing rules\r\n\r\nThese rules govern prose a developer reads, including chat replies, reports, guides, README files,\r\nand commit messages.\r\n`AGENTS.md` § Writing governs prose everywhere, and this file does not restate it; this file adds\r\nonly what a developer audience decides. An instruction file follows `AGENTS.md` § Instruction files\r\nfirst, and these rules wherever a rule here names no different form for it.\r\n\r\n## Voice and actor\r\n\r\n- Write `must` for a requirement, `can` for an option or an ability, and `might` for a possibility.\r\n  A recommendation takes the imperative, the same form `AGENTS.md` § Writing fixes for instructions.\r\n  Never write `should`, and never soften a recommendation into `We recommend`.\r\n- Address the developer as `you`. Name the software component that acts, and make it the subject of\r\n  the sentence. Reserve `user` for someone using software the developer builds. Never write `we`,\r\n  `our`, or `let's` about agent work.\r\n- Give software no human faculties. A component reports, returns, detects, or refuses; it never\r\n  knows, thinks, wants, or sees.\r\n- Use the passive voice only where naming the actor adds blame and nothing else, such as a defect\r\n  count. Name the actor wherever the reader needs it.\r\n- Use negative contractions (`don't`, `isn't`, `can't`) in a reply or a guide. Do not use them in an\r\n  instruction file.\r\n\r\n## Sentence and paragraph order\r\n\r\n- Put the condition, the goal, or the location before the instruction, so a reader the instruction\r\n  does not apply to stops at the first clause. Put the result or the reason after it. This governs a\r\n  sentence, not a document: a report still opens with its finding.\r\n- Put the key point in the first sentence of every paragraph and every list item, not only in the\r\n  opening of the reply or the section.\r\n- Keep the helper words `that`, `then`, `of`, `a`, and `the`. Do not drop one for brevity.\r\n- Name the noun after `this`, `these`, or `it` wherever the reader could attach the pronoun to\r\n  another referent.\r\n- State what the reader can do. Do not write a double negative.\r\n\r\n## Claims and time\r\n\r\n- Claim only what the reader can check. Never write `ensure`, `guarantee`, a superlative, or an\r\n  effort adjective as a claim about behavior, and cite the run behind every number. `ensure`\r\n  addressed to an executor as a directive is outside this ban.\r\n- Write the present tense for what exists. Do not write `currently`, `now`, `new`, `latest`, or\r\n  `soon`; where time matters, give the version or the date.\r\n- Recommend one path, the shortest one you proved. Rule on every option you list, and drop an option\r\n  equivalent to the one you recommend.\r\n\r\n## Code tokens, references, and links\r\n\r\n- Put a code token in backticks and follow it with a noun: the `parse` method, the `vite.config.ts`\r\n  file, the `--check` flag. Never inflect, pluralize, or possessivize a code token, and never use one\r\n  as an English verb.\r\n- Point to other material with `preceding`, `following`, `earlier`, or `later`, never with `above` or\r\n  `below`. Use `earlier` and `later` for a version range too.\r\n- Write link text as the destination's title or a descriptive phrase, introduced by `see`. Never\r\n  write `here`, `this document`, or a bare URL in prose.\r\n- Paraphrase third-party content and link its source; never paste it into prose you author. A\r\n  vendored mirror is fetched bytes rather than authored prose, and `.claude/rules/documentation.md`\r\n  governs it.\r\n\r\n## Structure\r\n\r\n- Keep a required fact in the main flow. A note or a notice carries only what the reader can skip,\r\n  never a prerequisite, a step, or a warning the task depends on.\r\n- In a reply or a guide, introduce every list, table, and code fence with a complete sentence naming\r\n  what follows; a rule file's list sits bare under its heading. Number a list only where order or\r\n  rank matters. Use a table only for rows with comparable fields.\r\n- Write a heading in sentence case, verb first for a task and a noun phrase for a concept. Identity\r\n  numbering — a claim, an audit verdict, a plan unit — is data rather than a heading style, and\r\n  stays.\r\n\r\n## Examples, numbers, and abbreviations\r\n\r\n- Build an example from fictional data with descriptive names and no personally identifiable\r\n  information. Write a placeholder in `UPPER_SNAKE_CASE` and explain it on first use. Never write\r\n  `foo`, `bar`, or `baz`.\r\n- Expand an abbreviation the reader may not know on first use, with the short form in parentheses.\r\n  Skip the expansion for one this audience reads daily, such as `API`, `CLI`, `JSON`, `URL`, or a\r\n  file format.\r\n- Write a numeral for a technical quantity, a version, or a measurement. Write a date as\r\n  `YYYY-MM-DD` in evidence, commit messages, and reports. See `AGENTS.md` § Writing for what\r\n  separates a value from a count.\r\n- Use the serial comma. Mark omitted code with a comment in the sample's language, never with `...`.\r\n\r\n## Substitutions\r\n\r\nReplace each term in this table with its replacement. A literal code identifier is data, and so is\r\na sample string inside a code fence or a test fixture: quote each as itself, exempt from every row.\r\n\r\n| Term                     | Replacement                               |\r\n| ------------------------ | ----------------------------------------- |\r\n| `should`                 | `must`, `can`, `might`, or the imperative |\r\n| `simply`, `easy`, `just` | Delete                                    |\r\n| `currently`, `now`       | Delete, or give the date                  |\r\n| `new`, `latest`          | Delete, or give the version               |\r\n| `utilize`, `leverage`    | `use`                                     |\r\n| `via`                    | `through`, `by using`                     |\r\n| `in order to`            | `to`                                      |\r\n| `e.g.`, `i.e.`           | `for example`, `that is`                  |\r\n| `etc.`                   | Bound the list, or recast the sentence    |\r\n| `performant`, `robust`   | The measured property                     |\r\n| `allows you to`          | `lets you`                                |\r\n| `and/or`                 | `and`, `or`, or `both`                    |\r\n| `since` (causal)         | `because`                                 |\r\n| `once` (temporal)        | `after`                                   |\r\n| `please`                 | Delete                                    |\r\n| `sanity check`           | `quick check`                             |\r\n| `dummy`                  | `placeholder`                             |\r\n| `blacklist`, `whitelist` | `denylist`, `allowlist`                   |\r\n| `master`, `slave`        | `primary`, `replica`                      |\r\n\r\n- `policy/no-banned-term` reads every comment and the prose sweep in `tests/setupPolicy.ts` reads\r\n  every authored Markdown file, and each matches the rows this table bans unconditionally. The rule\r\n  and the sweep leave `now`, `new`, `latest`, `once`, `since`, and `master` unmatched because those\r\n  rows carry a permitted sense, so rule a hit in one of those rows yourself.\r\n- Sweep case-insensitively and across inflections when checking prose against the preceding table. A\r\n  pattern for `easy` reaches neither `Easy` nor `easier`, and a temporal `once` most often appears\r\n  as a sentence-initial `Once`.\r\n- Rule every hit by the sense its row bans, not by the match. `once` counts as often as it means\r\n  `after`, and `new` names a value as often as it dates one. Record a hit in a permitted sense as\r\n  permitted rather than dropping it.\r\n- Name the pattern and the paths behind every sweep result, including a clean one. A result naming\r\n  neither reports on the population its pattern admitted rather than on the population it was drawn\r\n  from.\r\n- Write singular `they` for a person of unstated gender.\r\n- This table carries no row for `execute`, `abort`, `kill`, `terminate`, or `run`:\r\n  `.claude/rules/names.md` § Fixed lifecycle vocabulary owns those words, and no row here overrides\r\n  it.\r\n\r\n## Not adopted\r\n\r\n- Do not add conversational personality, warmth, or memorability to a reply; the prose stays plain.\r\n- Do not import a license to break these rules where a departure reads better; an exception exists\r\n  only where a rule states it.\r\n- `We recommend` as a recommendation form — refused in § Voice and actor.\r\n- Do not apply a source guide's sample indentation or line-length chrome; the repository formatter\r\n  and `.claude/rules/typescript.md` decide code presentation.\r\n- Do not write an unspaced em dash; this project spaces it.\r\n- Do not cap a sentence at a word count; `AGENTS.md` § Writing owns sentence length.\r\n- Do not treat an external word list as vocabulary law; the preceding substitution table is the\r\n  adopted set.\r\n---\r\npaths:\r\n  - 'src/**/*'\r\n  - 'app/**/*'\r\n  - 'configs/**/*'\r\n  - 'tests/**/*'\r\n  - 'scripts/**/*'\r\n  - 'guides/**/*'\r\n  - 'package.json'\r\n  - '.gitattributes'\r\n  - '.github/workflows/*'\r\n---\r\n\r\n# Host portability rules\r\n\r\nPublished source runs on Windows and Linux. Write one implementation that is correct on each host,\r\nor gate the host-specific branch on the host that needs it.\r\n\r\nTake the test half from `.claude/rules/tests.md`: it governs a host-varying property inside a test\r\nand the form of a conditional skip.\r\n\r\n## Host branching\r\n\r\n- Branch on a capability a runtime probe answers — a returned value, a thrown code, a present\r\n  member — wherever the difference is detectable.\r\n- Branch on `process.platform` only where the behavior is platform-defined, and name that fact in\r\n  the symbol's `@remarks`.\r\n- Put a platform-literal table — install root, executable suffix, toolchain name — inside a\r\n  `process.platform` branch, and read it on that platform alone.\r\n- Never flatten a correctly-gated host branch into a single path, and never relax a fail-closed\r\n  check to make one host pass.\r\n\r\n## Line endings\r\n\r\n- Split arrived text on `/\\r\\n|\\n/`. Never split on `\\n` alone, and never split on a bare `\\r`.\r\n- Split first, then trim each line; trimming the whole payload leaves a `\\r` on every line but the\r\n  last.\r\n- Frame a named protocol — HTTP, LSP, RFC 6455, NDJSON — with the terminator that protocol fixes,\r\n  and leave that terminator alone.\r\n- Emit `\\n` from code this package owns. Never emit `os.EOL`.\r\n\r\n## Paths\r\n\r\n- Normalize each operand of a path comparison with the same normalizer before comparing.\r\n- Never case-fold a containment check. Fold a lookup key only by the file-name case sensitivity the\r\n  consumer declares, and fold the whole key when you fold at all.\r\n- Compose, split, and relativize a host path with `node:path`. Never concatenate a separator\r\n  literal.\r\n- Keep a storage key, an archive key, and a URI path slash-separated, and refuse `\\` and a drive\r\n  prefix inside one.\r\n- Refuse the Windows reserved device names and the filename characters Windows rejects, on every\r\n  host.\r\n- Read the temporary directory from `os.tmpdir()`. Never write a `/tmp` literal in source.\r\n- Build a `file:` URI with `pathToFileURL`. Never format one from a path string.\r\n- Convert a `file:` URI to a host path with `fileURLToPath`. Never read `URL.pathname` as\r\n  a host path or strip the scheme, decode escapes, or rewrite a drive prefix by hand.\r\n\r\n## Processes and executables\r\n\r\n- Resolve, spawn, and terminate a child through `@orkestrel/process` where the package declares it.\r\n- Where it is not declared, spawn `process.execPath` with a JavaScript entry. Never spawn a `.bin`\r\n  shim, and never add `shell: true` to reach one.\r\n- Take a resolver's first match only after splitting its output into lines and trimming each one.\r\n  `where` prints a match per line and can name a file that is not an executable.\r\n- Treat `fs.constants.X_OK` as an existence check on Windows, where `accessSync` passes on a plain\r\n  file. Confirm an executable by its name and its file type instead.\r\n- Read an `fs.constants` member before putting it in a flag word. Windows leaves `O_NOFOLLOW`\r\n  undefined, so `O_WRONLY | O_NOFOLLOW` refuses nothing there. Never polyfill the missing member:\r\n  compensate with a check the host supports, or record the limit.\r\n- Read `PROGRAMFILES`, `LOCALAPPDATA`, and `APPDATA` inside the win32 branch and `HOME` inside the\r\n  POSIX branch. Never read one host's root on the other.\r\n- Terminate a Windows child by its process tree. A `SIGTERM` handler never runs there, so gate every\r\n  cooperative-shutdown path on POSIX.\r\n- Treat a permission bit as advisory on Windows: a written mode does not round-trip\r\n  (`chmodSync(dir, 0o500)` reads back `0444` there), so assert modes only where a probe reports they\r\n  round-trip.\r\n- Retry an `EBUSY`, `ENOTEMPTY`, or `EPERM` removal inside a bounded budget, and report the failure\r\n  when the budget ends.\r\n\r\n## Terminals\r\n\r\n- Gate styling and interactivity on `isTTY`, `NO_COLOR`, and `FORCE_COLOR`, never on the platform.\r\n- Decode `\\r`, `\\n`, and `\\r\\n` from a key stream as the same key.\r\n- Never call a Windows console-mode API. Node exposes none, so record the unreached behavior as a\r\n  limit.\r\n\r\n## Scripts and packaging\r\n\r\n- Write every `package.json` script as a portable command: a Node invocation or an installed binary.\r\n  Never name a `.sh` file there.\r\n- Keep `#!/usr/bin/env node` on an npm bin. npm writes the Windows shim from it.\r\n- Keep an agent hook under `scripts/` in bash, and keep `.gitattributes` `eol=lf`.\r\n\r\n## Claims\r\n\r\n- Never describe the suite, the host, or CI as POSIX. Name the host and the date behind a reading.\r\n- Run the gate chain on each host a change claims, and record a reading that could not run as a\r\n  limit naming the host it needs.\r\n- Keep a POSIX-shaped `file:///` literal in a guide only as an opaque fictional identifier. Build\r\n  any example a reader executes with `pathToFileURL`.\r\n","exit_code":0,"status":"completed"}}

Reasoning separation, the authority gate, and durable jobs sit around that boundary. The model itself is the one thing this package does not supply: `ProviderInterface` is a contract, not an implementation, so any backend that satisfies it drops in unchanged and the host application decides which one. Nor is there hidden global state, a plugin lifecycle, a prompt-template DSL, or an implicit memory store. This is a kit of composable primitives: the loop is the convenient way to use them, not the only one, and a caller that would rather bound and drive a provider by hand can skip it entirely.\nguides/agent.md:16:## Surface\nguides/agent.md:18:The agent-owned surface: the inference boundary, the conversation layer, the context and its managers, the loop, the authority gate, and the durable-job bridge. Their readonly data members remain Surface rows. `ThinkSplitter`, `InstructionManager`, `Scope`, `ScopeManager`, `AgentContext`, `Agent`, `Authority`, `AgentRegistry`, `Conversation`, and `ConversationManager` implement their interfaces exactly, so the tables also describe those classes' instance methods. The store classes implement `ConversationStoreInterface` and keep explicit tables because their class names have no same-name interface contracts. `MessageManagerInterface` has no separate concrete class here: the active `Conversation` satisfies it structurally. A host application supplies the concrete `ProviderInterface`. Tool and workspace methods live in their dependency guides.\nguides/agent.md:592:#### `ProviderInterface`\nguides/agent.md:601:#### `ThinkSplitterInterface`\nguides/agent.md:603:The stream-stateful `<think>…</think>` separator a provider routes raw content deltas through, so it yields clean content and surfaces the reasoning as `ProviderResult.thinking`. The `content` / `thinking` data members (the authoritative clean-content + reasoning accumulations) stay Surface rows — `content` matters because some chat templates pre-seed `<think>` into the prompt scaffold (the qwen3 shape), so only a bare `</think>` ever appears on the wire: before any tag event, that bare close reclassifies everything surfaced so far into `thinking` (one-shot — afterwards a bare close is plain text), correcting `content` retroactively where the already-returned deltas cannot be recalled. One splitter serves one stream — create a fresh one per call (`createThinkSplitter`).\nguides/agent.md:610:#### `MessageManagerInterface`\nguides/agent.md:612:The immutable conversation store. `add` mints each message's `id` and carries batch overloads (one input → one message, a batch → the array); `remove` carries batch overloads (one or a list). The `count` data member stays a Surface row.\nguides/agent.md:622:#### `InstructionManagerInterface`\nguides/agent.md:624:The name-keyed instruction registry a richer context renders a directives block from. `add` mints each `id` and carries batch overloads (a re-`add` of the same name overwrites it, last write wins); `remove` carries batch overloads. The `emitter` / `count` / `open` / `format` data members stay Surface rows.\nguides/agent.md:635:#### `ContextSectionSourceInterface`\nguides/agent.md:637:The manager surface one section's format cascade reads. `render` is its only method — the `open` (the built-in header) and `format` (the raw manager-options override) data members stay Surface rows. An `InstructionManagerInterface` satisfies it structurally, which is what lets `resolveOpen` / `resolveClose` / `resolveItem` stay independent of which manager supplies the section.\nguides/agent.md:643:#### `ScopeInterface`\nguides/agent.md:645:The named, immutable allow-list filter. `narrow` is the only method — the `id` / `name` data members and the per-category allow-lists stay Surface rows.\nguides/agent.md:651:#### `ScopeManagerInterface`\nguides/agent.md:653:The id-keyed registry of reusable named scopes. `create` mints + stores a scope (always adds — never overwrites, since two scopes may share a `name`); `remove` carries batch overloads. The `emitter` / `count` data members stay Surface rows.\nguides/agent.md:663:#### `AgentContextInterface`\nguides/agent.md:665:The richer turn context. `apply` changes the active per-turn filter and `build` assembles the provider input. The `system` / `instructions` / `messages` / `tools` / `scope` / `workspaces` / `conversations` readonly data members stay Surface rows.\nguides/agent.md:672:#### `AgentInterface`\nguides/agent.md:674:The bounded agent loop. `generate` and `stream` share one private run (`generate` drains the same stream `stream` exposes, so they can't diverge); `abort` cancels the in-flight turn. The `emitter` / `id` / `status` / `context` data members stay Surface rows (`emitter` is a `readonly` accessor — a property, not a method).\nguides/agent.md:682:#### `StreamInterface`\nguides/agent.md:690:#### `ChannelInterface`\nguides/agent.md:718:#### `AuthorityInterface`\nguides/agent.md:726:#### `AgentRegistryInterface`\nguides/agent.md:732:| `provider`  | `ProviderInterface`  | Resolves a registered `ProviderInterface` by name — throws `unknown provider: <name>` when absent.                                                                                                                    |\nguides/agent.md:738:#### `ConversationInterface`\nguides/agent.md:740:A conversation that owns its live message tail directly (the flat store verbs folded in, like a `Workspace` owns its files). `add` mints each message's `id` and stores it (batch overloads); `message` / `messages` look up the live tail; `remove` / `clear` drop from it. `view` is the model input; `compact` folds the older live messages into a summarized `Section` (regenerating the rollup, emitting `summary` then `compact`); `rehydrate` / `search` read the retained originals; `reference` renders this conversation as a provenance-labeled block to pull into another (a pure string, no model call). The `id` / `emitter` / `summary` / `sections` / `count` data members stay Surface rows (`emitter` is a `readonly` accessor — a property, not a method).\nguides/agent.md:756:#### `ConversationManagerInterface`\nguides/agent.md:758:The id-keyed registry of `Conversation`s with an active pointer. `add(input?)` mints a conversation (flowing the manager's default `summarize` / `keep` in unless the input overrides them) and auto-activates the first one; a later `add` leaves `active` unchanged. `switch(id)` re-points `active` (an unknown `id` returns `undefined`, leaving `active` unchanged — lenient, never throws); `remove` carries batch overloads (the array overload first) and clears `active` when the removed conversation was active. The `count` and `active` data members stay Surface rows (`active` is a `readonly` accessor — a property, not a method); the manager is event-free (each conversation owns its `emitter`).\nguides/agent.md:771:#### `ConversationStoreInterface`\nguides/agent.md:781:#### `MemoryConversationStore`\nguides/agent.md:791:#### `DatabaseConversationStore`\nguides/agent.md:801:## Contract\nguides/agent.md:805:1. **Doc ↔ source bijection.** Every `function` / `class` / `const` / `interface` / `type` row in the `## Surface` tables is a real export of `src/core`, and every export appears as a Surface row — exhaustive, both directions.\nguides/agent.md:806:2. **`ProviderInterface` is the abstract inference boundary.** A provider turns a conversation (plus optional `tools`, a non-empty `ToolDefinition[]`) into a turn: `generate` resolves the assembled `ProviderResult`, `stream` yields channel-tagged `ProviderDelta`s and returns the assembled result. Both methods accept optional `ProviderStreamOptions`; `think` is the per-call reasoning override. It carries an `id` (a per-instance trace label) and `name` (the backend identifier). This module defines only the contract — a concrete implementation is a host application's responsibility.\nguides/agent.md:807:3. **`stream` yields deltas + returns the assembled result.** A `ProviderInterface.stream` yields each non-empty answer delta as `{ channel: 'content', text }` and each native live reasoning delta as `{ channel: 'thinking', text }`; its return value is the assembled `ProviderResult` whose `content` is the provider's authoritative clean answer, plus any tool calls and usage the turn reported. So a caller can render tokens and reasoning live while still recovering the complete outcome from the generator's return value. A thinking model's reasoning is separated at the provider, never assembled as content: a concrete provider routes its raw wire content through a `ThinkSplitter` (`createThinkSplitter` — one per stream), yields the clean content, assembles `content` from the splitter's authoritative accumulation (an implicit pre-seeded open — the qwen3-template bare `</think>` — reclassifies the already-yielded prefix into thinking, the one shape where live content deltas can transiently over-report), and surfaces the accumulated reasoning as `ProviderResult.thinking` — which never re-enters the conversation (the `Agent` joins it across a run's calls onto `AgentResult.thinking` as display/audit metadata).\nguides/agent.md:814:10. **The agent loop (`Agent` / `createAgent`).** `Agent` composes a `ProviderInterface`, an `AgentContext`, and its `@orkestrel/tool` registry into the bounded context → provider → tools → repeat turn. It builds the provider input, then iterates up to `limit`: stream a provider turn, accumulate content/thinking/usage, append any assistant tool calls, and execute them. Each discriminated `ToolResult` becomes a tool message whose content is `JSON.stringify(result.value)` when `result.success` is true and `result.error` when false, so failure text is never JSON-quoted. A final assistant turn stops the loop.\nguides/agent.md:818:14. **Pull and push observation surfaces on the `Agent`; the rest event-free.** The provider contract, the tool registry, the conversation store, and the `AgentContext` itself carry no Emitter, no `EventMap`, no `on` hook — they stay purely functional (of the managers the context composes, the `InstructionManager` and `ScopeManager` carry their own `emitter`s and each `Conversation` owns one, while the `ToolManager`, `WorkspaceManager`, and `ConversationManager` are event-free — as is the context that composes them). The `Agent` itself carries both a pull and a push surface. Pull: the `AgentChunk` stream (`stream().events`) yields per-token answer deltas, per-think reasoning deltas, usage chunks, and tool chunks for a live consumer. Push: the `emitter` (`AgentEventMap`) — `start` (run begins), `turn` (each iteration), `tool` (a dispatched call + result), `usage` (a turn's usage), `deny` (an authority denial — not in the chunk stream), `finish` (the settled result), `error` (a genuine failure), `abort` (a cancel), `exhaust` (a limit exhausted with unresolved tool intent — fires instead of `abort`), and `fault` (a non-fatal automatic-compaction summarizer throw — the run continues; the automatic-compaction clause) — wired through the emitter pattern (`AgentOptions.on` hooks, the `AgentOptions.error` listener-error handler, a `readonly emitter`, `new Emitter({ on: options?.on, error: options?.error })`). Per-token / per-thinking deltas are the stream's job exclusively — there is deliberately no `token` or `think` event. The emitter isolates a listener throw (it can never escape into the settle-once / wake-park loop) and routes it to its own `error` handler (the `error` option, surfaced as `(error, event)`, not a domain event; itself guarded against re-entrancy), so observation is provably side-effect-free on the 3×-hardened loop — and every emit sits after the relevant state transition / settle, so it cannot reorder control flow. A cancelled run emits `abort` (the cancel reason) then `finish` (the settled partial); a genuine error emits `error` instead of `finish`. The loop's deterministic logic is pinned in the `src:core` mirror with a scripted `ProviderInterface`.\nguides/agent.md:819:15. **Doc ↔ source method bijection.** The `## Methods` tables list exactly the public methods of every agent-owned interface named there, exhaustive in both directions, and each agent-owned concrete class exposes the same public methods as its interface. `MessageManagerInterface` is implemented structurally by the active `Conversation`; `ProviderInterface` is implemented by the host. Dependency method surfaces belong to [`tool.md`](tool.md) and [`workspace.md`](workspace.md).\nguides/agent.md:844:## Patterns\nguides/agent.md:846:### Bounding any provider call\nguides/agent.md:848:`ProviderInterface.generate` / `.stream` take a plain `AbortSignal`, so fold an [abort](abort.md), a [timeout](timeout.md), and a token [budget](budget.md) into one bound through `AbortSignal.any` — whichever trips first cancels the call. This works for any provider; constructing the concrete one is a host application's job.\nguides/agent.md:851:import type { ProviderInterface } from '@orkestrel/agent'\nguides/agent.md:856:declare the system prompt prepended + tools advertised structurally; tool iteration (turn 1's `ToolCall` → `execute` → the result fed back → turn 2's final content); a tool throw fed back as the tool message (the loop never throws); `generate` ↔ `stream` parity (same script → deep-equal); the `AgentChunk` sequence (`token` / `think` / `usage` / `tool` chunks, with the tool chunk carrying the executed call + result and usage summed); per-run `think` forwarding into the provider; the iteration cap (an always-tool script stops at `limit`); abort (a pre-aborted `signal` commits a partial without calling the provider; `abort()` mid-stream resolves `partial: true` with the accumulated content; a genuine provider error rejects); the token `budget` bound (exhausted usage stops the turn, `partial`); the `scheduler` yielding between turns (not after the last); `status` transitions; the authority gate wired into the loop (no authority → unchanged; an allowed call executes; a denied call is not executed [a counter tool proves it] yet a `tool` chunk + tool message carry the denial and the next provider call sees it; a mixed batch merges allowed + denied in original call order; an all-denied turn feeds back denials and the cap still bounds it); and the scope-filtered tool advertisement (no scope → all tools advertised; a `tools` allow-list → only the listed definitions reach the provider, a scoped-out tool absent on every turn so its handler never runs — neither described nor callable; an empty `tools` list → the provider is handed `undefined`); and automatic compaction (the context `window` budget): the between-turns trigger (the absolute prompt crossing `max` fires `compact()` + rebuilds smaller, exactly twice over the script, the run answering through the compacted view), the no-fire-below-the-window guard, and the additive regressions (no `window` ⇒ never folds; no conversation ⇒ the trigger is skipped + the budget untouched); and the production hardening (a long-conversation initial prompt compacted pre-first-turn so the first provider call sees the compacted view; a throwing auto-summarizer caught + surfaced as `fault` with the run continuing to a valid answer while a manual `compact()` still throws; the futile guard — a `compact()` that folds nothing while over the window latches per-run so auto-compaction stops, no churn); and the multi-conversation pattern (one agent + a `ConversationManager`, switching the active conversation with `agent.context.conversations.switch(id)` per request: independent accumulated histories with no cross-talk, and independent per-thread compaction whose sections retain only their own thread's originals).\nguides/agent.md:1203:## See also\nconfigs/policy.ts:138:\t'contracts.ts',\nconfigs/policy.ts:180:\t'contracts.ts',\nconfigs/policy.ts:193:export const DATA_EXEMPT_FILES: readonly string[] = Object.freeze(['helpers.ts'])\nconfigs/policy.ts:949:\tif (DATA_SOURCE_FILES.includes(file) || DATA_EXEMPT_FILES.includes(file)) return\nconfigs/policy.ts:1234:\t\t\t\t'Disallow a non-const, non-UPPER_SNAKE_CASE, or bare-collection declaration in constants.ts.',\nconfigs/policy.ts:1238:\t\t\tnaming: 'Name every constants.ts declaration in UPPER_SNAKE_CASE.',\nwarning: unable to access 'C:\\Users\\mikes/.config/git/ignore': Permission denied\nwarning: unable to access 'C:\\Users\\mikes/.config/git/ignore': Permission denied\n","exit_code":0,"status":"completed"}}

The policy sweep\r\n  (`tests/setupPolicy.ts`) takes the rules that are path- or text-shaped, and every rule whose\r\n  subject is suppression itself.\r\n- Choose between them by what can defeat the rule: an instrument must not be suppressible by the\r\n  thing it polices. A file-level `oxlint-disable` silently defeats every lint rule in its file,\r\n  plugin rules included, and nothing inside a file can suppress the sweep.\r\n- Write each visitor in the plugin's visitor table as a one-line context-binding arrow delegating to\r\n  a named module-scope `report{Noun}` function. This section fixes the instruments and how work is assigned\r\n  between them; each rule's substance stays with the law it enforces.\r\n\r\n## Text integrity\r\n\r\n- Store text as UTF-8.\r\n- Before accepting broad generated or migrated edits, scan changed text for replacement characters, mojibake, unintended control characters, and accidental trailing debris.\r\n- Preserve intentional Unicode punctuation and symbols; do not “clean” valid text merely because it is non-ASCII.\r\n- Never renormalize Unicode while rewriting a file. Retyping a line can silently fold a decomposed sequence into its precomposed form — `e` + U+0301 becoming U+00E9 — and the two render identically, so the diff reads as a no-op and review sees nothing. Where the exact code points are the subject, as in an encoding or transport proof, that fold deletes the case the test exists for while leaving it green and named. Move such a line rather than retyping it, and compare bytes with `od -c` or a code-point dump rather than by eye.\r\n","exit_code":0,"status":"completed"}}
